## THE ANSWER, IN ONE PARAGRAPH (for the DM)

The world grows three kinds of body on its own, and each wants something different: a **conclave** (the small flock of a faith — the faith ladder's `cult` standing, never villainy) wants recognition and growth and acts as its god's character leans; a **gang** wants coin and safety and thrives where the town's law looks away; a **beast** pack wants food and territory, is hostile to everyone, and breathes with the seasons. None appears from nothing: each is born from a fact the world already holds — a town's quarter spilling out, a den's holder, the wild in its season, a routed levy's deserters, migrants turned away — and its card says which. They move on the world's ONE road ladder like everyone — its lowest rungs are the hidden paths through forest, marsh and mountain that the map draws and use wears in — and each week a body CHOOSES road or path (a hunted gang leans to the path, a welcome flock to the road, a hungry winter pack comes down onto the road), sorties onto the road at the junctions, and lives off what it takes from the villages, hamlets and thorps whose walls and watch are thin (a walled town is no prey until its walls are breached) ; supplies are their readiness, their size and their very existence, and a held site is their home base and cohesion. They meet each other, the patrols, the caravans, the villages and the armies, and every such engagement is a hearing in its week. Their presence is what an embattled region is made of — the gauge stays the one gauge, the bodies feed it — so killing them makes a region QUIET, while only fixing what breeds them (the watch, the wages, the keep, the known trails, the bread) makes it SAFE. A body has no power of its own: its deeds move the standings of the towns, the courts, the faiths and the nobles who hold the sword. The engine never sends a body toward the party, never names a body's purpose, and never turns one into a menace; the DM may promote any body into a menace with one act, and may end, pin or re-stance any of them.

Over a body's life : a small body is NAMELESS ("brigands on the Tolle road") until it grows past the size at which it could raid a hamlet; then it takes a name from its place and kind, towns begin to keep word of it, and a court may post a BOUNTY on it — the call board's hook for the table's heroes and villains. A peaceable flock ASKS ALMS of every town in its reach and gets what the town's god and purse allow; it may take an empty site or clear a brigands' den without being pursued, and a court that wants the place back may buy it out. Gangs and flocks cannot mend a fort past a modest state on their own; beasts never mend anything. Every body LEARNS (the stretch where it was caught, the village that paid, the riders' rhythm), and so do the towns and their riders — the habitual one is caught or evaded. A court's public-security office may lead a HUNT; a warden who holds that office knows the hidden ways of its land. A gang has ONE end: to scatter rich, to found a company of swords or a guild where none exists (far from where it is known), or to make a hamlet of the site it held so long; a guild war is what happens when it tries to overthrow the guild that already rules the quarter. A foreign court may SPONSOR a gang or a flock in secret — by a courier, a smuggler's load, a cache or a mother temple's alms — and the day the silver is found, the blame, the casus and the hunt all turn on the sponsor. A captive taken by a body — a gang's, or a hostile flock's when its god allows — is RACED for: the body that survives the ransom's clock and is paid keeps the price; the body beaten first loses the captive free; a body neither paid nor beaten lets him go . Every one of these is a card the DM may open any week until it resolves on its own, and every act drives the crises the world already knows — never a new one.

### 0.2 The binding prior words
- **The deity doctrine** (ADDENDUM 15): faith = culture, never theological; THE FOLLOWERS ACT, THE GOD DOES NOT; the derived temper (the stored temper is INERT); a creed's SETTLEMENT standing (`cult · established · ascendant`) and pantheon rank are different facts.
- **RULINGS-ROUND3 §D.2 (corrected by the owner, 17:27):** a cult is the small end of the faith ladder (a SIZE standing), never villainy; a band is a criminal body; one actor idiom, different wants; the slot was WC-12's — THIS VOLUME FILLS IT.
- **MN12 (the owner, 01:3x: "menaces are DM authored only, never organically emergent"):** the engine never spawns a menace; WC-12's located brigand body is the ambient raider stressor's LOCATED form; ONE `raiding` word.
- **WC §1.8.1 (designed, unlanded):** a brigand free unit is LOCATED; `EMBATTLED_RESOLUTIONS = ['suppression', 'worn_down', 'absorbed', 'buy_off']`; "a brigand siege is ONE MORE RAMP TERM on embattlement.js, never a parallel stressor" (the fold's §3.1.16 ruling) — that term IS THE BODIES' presence row (§8.2), one term per gang, never two .
- **RULINGS-ROUND3 A.1 · A.3 · A.7 · A.10 / E.1:** the keep's garrison is never the town's riders; the presence composer is a SHARED car (first of C14 · C15 · C16 · C10 builds it; every later feeder registers — CHAIR-NOTES 18 (d) makes every shared leaf an OWNED unit with one manifest row); the actor convention's kinds (SIX since RULINGS-ROUND3 §G.3 and RULINGS-ROUND4 A.12: `settlement · person · party · menace · corps · body`, `bodyKind` a field; §2.1, OD-BD-3) ; ST's readiness words `at_strength · manned · sparse · skeleton · hollow`.
- **ST (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_ST.md` §2.1, as round 4a cured it under RULINGS-ROUND4 A.3):** an `untied` holder's ref is `{ kind: 'menace', id }` or THE BODIES' `{ kind: 'body', bodyKind: 'gang' | 'beast', id }` (an id-less authored gang before C17); a CONCLAVE holds as ST's `faith` holder with ref `{ kind: 'body', bodyKind: 'conclave', id }`; `cult` and `band` are never holder kinds (`cult` stays the faith ladder's standing — one word, one meaning) ; "empty = no effect" (ST7); no `lair` kind.
- **PR (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_PR.md`):** §5.3's `patrol_engages` row now reads THE BODIES' body row and §5.5 THE HUNT (the round-4a text; the compile's quote "a brigand body (WC-12, slot) or an untied band on the road" no longer exists) ; §8.1 the ONE encounter law `patrolEncounter` ("A BODY ON THE ROAD, ON A TRAIL, IN THE COUNTRY … a traveller ITS OWN STAGE moves"); §8.3 ONE believed-watch read `believedWatch`; the placement digest's `lived` rows (`lived: { forTick, on: [{ road, holder }…] }`), the fact of where riders stood.
- **WC §1.8.1 (designed):** "Against the town's defense + internal security -- both read through defenseLedger(settlement), the ONE read-point" — BD14's read .
- **PS1:** passage movers are `army · column · patrol · corps` — "NEVER a `shed_column` …, a refugee or migrant column": a body never asks passage.
- **CH (§9, §16b):** the clandestine hire (`covert: true`, the leak through RN's `TELLING_SOURCES`, exposure through `corruptionWeb.js :: applyForeignExposureBlowback` source `covert_hire_exposed`); the corps' failure rung `desertion`.
- **WF-5b (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_WF.md` §3):** "`.deities[ref].covert` … { share(band), sinceTick, shepherdRef? } (J-WF-4: in place, never a parallel ledger)" — the faith's own underground; an outlawed conclave that HIDES in town is this, never a new record.
- **The fold §3.4:** the registry already holds `raid` ("the war volume (border incidents) | the raid's landing | `repelled · succeeds · bought_off` | CLIMAX"), `supply_raid` (`repelled · succeeds`) and `caravan_seized` (WY-6, `seized · ransomed · passed`); "`country` none heard today — a menace's raiding is its stressor's ordinary life, not a hearing".
- **THE PROMISE:** a seed is a starting world forever; tuning is owner-signed — the re-feed (§8.5) is a declared same-seed shift, the owner's to sign at its unit.

### 0.3 The population facts that bound the design
- Embattlement is CANON-ONLY (`embattlementActive`: "Embattlement is LIVE iff the spatial-canon marker is present"); the bodies' land arms are therefore canon-only, and an aspatial world keeps exactly today's behaviour (§13 TIER). The CONCLAVE's in-town choice rides the faith row and is aspatial-safe.
- Danger is NODE-KEYED until the physical world lands (MN14); a body's WHERE is a country, a road stretch (`stretch:<a|b>`), a site, or a trail on the derived hidden graph (§5).
- The world's towns are FEW (the belief envelope 5–30 settlements; `armyTransit.js :: assertArmyBound`); ONE CAP per country bounds the bodies to a handful per region (§3.4), so every per-body pass is O(bodies × neighbours).
- The people ledger has no callers: a body is a BAND, not a headcount (macro over micro); no census is debited by a birth until WC's ledger runs (OD-PR-32's precedent: no population debit for a cut-down patrol).

---

## §1 THE LAWS (BD1–BD28)

**BD1 — ONE IDIOM, THREE KINDS, THREE WANTS.** A body is one record shape on one ledger (§2.2) of three closed kinds — `conclave · gang · beast` — each with its own want (conclave: recognition and growth, weighted by its god; gang: coin and safety, weighted by the town's law; beast: food and territory, hostile to all), its own closed choice set (§4), its own source of supplies (§6) and its own births (§3). Everything else is shared: one weekly choice drawn by the seeded stream from a situation-scored set, one physics, one encounter read, one band contest, one stance grammar, one ledger writer. The three are related by law, never by coincidence (§7.2, §9): gangs and conclaves compete for the same desperate people; an outlawed conclave hides the way a gang does but stays a church; beasts lose ground as towns grow while gangs gain it as the watch thins; all three are what an embattled region is made of, are hunted by the same riders and take the same sites. 

**BD2 — NEVER FROM NOTHING; NEVER FOR NOTHING.** Every body is born from a fact the world already holds, through one of FIVE births (§3.2) — a settlement's spill, a den's holder, the wild in its season, a defeat's deserters, migrants cast out — and carries a BIRTHPLACE RECEIPT (`birth`, the Evidence Law: "formed from the deserters of Kell's levy in the Tolle hills"). A body also ends from a fact (§6.4, §6.8, §6.9): starved, scattered, dissolved (into a town's faith row, or — a gang's founding end — into an institution or a hamlet), dispersed rich into the towns, merged into another, ranged away beyond the world's reach, raised into a menace by the DM, or disbanded by the DM — receipted . No body has a purpose, a destiny or a target the DM did not give it (the owner, 01:3x: "they have no purpose or destiny unless directed by the user").

**BD3 — A CONCLAVE IS A ROW OR A BODY, NEVER BOTH.** A creed's small flock in a town IS the creed's landed row in that town at `cult` standing (`worldState.religionStates[cid].deities[ref]`), given a weekly choice that is DERIVED and never stored (§4.2): no field is added to the row. A conclave becomes a MOVING body only when it leaves — driven out by its court or sent forth on a mission (§3.2 (a)) — and then it holds only what a row cannot (where, size, supplies, cohesion); it holds NO share, standing or legitimacy anywhere while it moves. It returns to the row by the faith system's own door (`religionState.js :: attemptEntry`) and its body record ends `dissolved`. One creed's people at one place are counted once. 

**BD4 — THE FOLLOWERS ACT; THE GOD DOES NOT.** A conclave's choice weights and every stance default that names a god read the deities' recorded axes through the landed `deityStance.js :: stanceOf` (aggression · cooperation) and the DERIVED temper `deityAxes.js :: deityTemper` (the stored temper is inert); no rule judges a god, writes a pantheon rank, or reads a creed's theology (faith = culture). A cult is never villainy: a good flock refuses extortion, warns the watch, shelters the hunted and fights a brigand that terrorizes the people at its size, even in a town whose god it opposes (the owner, ~18:5x). 

**BD5 — ONE TRUTH PER QUANTITY; THE BODIES COMPOSE INTO EACH.** The bodies never keep a second copy of any quantity the world already has; they compose INTO the one read: the embattled level through ONE registered row of MN §3.3's `presenceTerms` (§8.2); a town's criminal power through ONE composed read over the landed `criminalStrength01Of` (`criminalPowerOf`, §7.3 — never into the stamp); a creed's standing through the one strength seam of `religiousContest.js :: advanceReligionStates` (§7.4); a road's loss through `banditryLoss` (§9.6); a migrant's road death through `roadDeathRate` (§9.6); an army's supply through the supply shipments' own banditry (§9.8); a site's holder through ST's one writer (§9.4); an envoy on a bandit road through the roads gauntlet's T3 arm (§9.5); a ransom through the landed captor and home answers (§9.10); a lesson through HB's one habit ledger (§4.4); a founded company or guild through the institution lifecycle's one writer (§6.8); a crisis through the stressor the world already knows (§22) . No second danger, crime, loss, battle, decay, reputation, conversion, habit, ransom or crisis law exists in this volume.

**BD6 — KEEP THE GAUGE, REPLACE THE ENGINE.** The embattled level (`embattlement.js`, its hysteresis and its persisted ledger) stays the ONE read every landed consumer uses — road losses, routing, migration deaths, supply, the dossier, the map's danger legend — untouched. The bodies CAUSE it: their presence, size and this week's acts are the road-country row of the one composition; the level feeds back as HABITAT (§3.1); internal security is the COUNTERFORCE that shrinks habitat and kills bodies. The number and size of bodies in a country is habitat minus security under one cap (§3.4); events are what those bodies DO — never a multiplier on the level. Dark, the landed ramp (crime, security, ST's dens, the raider stressor) is the whole engine, byte-identical (the DEGRADED ARM).

**BD7 — QUIET IS NOT SAFE.** Two facts with two words on scrutiny: the EMBATTLED LEVEL is what is present and acting — when the bodies are gone it eases on its own hysteresis (`EXIT_THRESHOLD` 0.30 after `MIN_DWELL_TICKS` 6; entry is `ENTER_THRESHOLD` 0.55) — and the HABITAT (low security, unrest, famine, cover country, unknown trails) is untouched by killing. Births read habitat with their own dwell and scale to it, always from one of the five sources, never in the week a body died. Killing bodies buys a season of quiet; fixing the habitat (wages, riders, a keep, the trails learned, bread) stops the births. The DM's scrutiny line shows both: "The hill roads are quiet; the quarter still breeds."

**BD8 — POWER STAYS IN INSTITUTIONS.** A body has NO power number. Its deeds and its defeat move STANDINGS that already exist: a gang's standing in a town (its own record's map, composed into the criminal power), the watch's and the court's legitimacy through the landed verdict and legitimacy reads, a creed's share through the faith system's own shares, the sword-holder's power share through the faction plane (§10.3). A body never writes internal security; the loop runs through the landed chain (embattled → trade losses → prosperity → wages → the watch), slow, with its counterforces.

**BD9 — SUPPLIES ARE READINESS, SIZE AND EXISTENCE; A SITE IS COHESION AND A HOME.** Each body holds ONE supply stock (`bodySupply01`: loot and food for a gang, alms and the faithful's gifts for a conclave, kills for a beast) with an upkeep by size; it is the body's FIGHT READINESS (`bodyReadiness01`), its SIZE (a provisioned body recruits from habitat, a starving one sheds) and its EXISTENCE (below a floor it scatters, dissolves or starves — receipted). A raid is a supply act first, so hunting the raids IS starving the body. A HELD SITE gives COHESION (`bodyCohesion01` decays slowly with a base, fast without) and is the base of a BOUNDED OPERATING RADIUS in weeks of travel (§5.4); a body without a site is ROAMING, short-lived. Moving the base is a choice, never a drift.

**BD10 — BEASTS ARE HOSTILE TO ALL, AND SEASONAL.** A beast's stance toward every other actor is the landed label `hostile` (hunger); every other kind's stance toward a beast is `hunt` or `avoid`; every court hunts beasts. A beast makes NO demands, holds NO ties, takes NO bribes, has NO court stance, is never permitted or employed, takes NO captive, repairs NOTHING, has NO leader and NO name; the engine never authors a beast for a purpose: the DM may PLACE a NAMELESS pack (`PLACE_BODY`, §11 — an ambient fact, the world's own from the week after), and a pack the DM NAMES or DRIVES is a menace (BD16) . Its supplies are KILLS (livestock on the land, travellers, migrants, patrols at its size); it breeds only in spring, hunts through the year, is driven to the roads by winter hunger, and starves when the kills stop — so the beast population breathes with the year. 

**BD11 — BOTH NETWORKS; THE BODY CHOOSES EACH MOVE; STANCE IS A PRIOR WEIGHT, NEVER A PROHIBITION.**  Every body may use EITHER the overt roads or the hidden ways (the one road ladder's lowest rungs, §5.2) and CHOOSES per move by its seeded situation roll (§5.3; the stream `bodies:route:<bodyId>:<tick>`), the receipt naming the choice on scrutiny ("the Hollow Road gang risked the Tolle road: the drover's way was watched"). The weights, each bounded `[0.25, 4]` with its reason (§4.1's idiom): the land-holder's STANCE toward it as BELIEVED (`believedStanceOf`) — a PRIOR: `curbed · outlawed · hunted` lean to the path, `permitted · endowed · tolerated · employed · harboured` to the road (a hunted gang still risks the road when the path is longer or its junction believed watched; a tolerated flock takes a path to dodge a toll); BELIEVED COVERAGE on each (PR §8.3 `believedWatch`, the way's known-and-watched mark as the body believes it, §2.3); the way's CAPACITY (a body above the band has no path, §5.2); COST (a path is slower — the landed ×2, beasts ×1; the season's cost on both); URGENCY (a pursued or a starving body); its HABITS (§4.4: a stretch it was caught on); and what the path passes AROUND (the owner, 22:15: "to avoid military and armies and tolls and gates") — the riders, an ARMY on the march (PS12's enemy-column presence read as a danger weight on the road), the territory holder's TOLL and the GATE's stance (PS's ladder; plague-R7's quarantine). A permitted conclave walks the road like any group of people and is never pursued; an employed gang rides the road in its employer's land. A BEAST takes the path at its own pace (cover is its ground: ×1, the owner's 22:19 "slower, except for beasts") and uses the ROAD as the easier ground — winter brings the pack down onto it (the classic encounter). A body never asks passage (PS1's movers are units; a body's crossing is THE PATROLS' encounter, never a PS hearing). 

**BD12 — BELIEF NEVER TRUTH.** A body knows what its TIES have heard: a gang plans on its home town's and its fence's belief, a moving conclave on its creed's seat's, through THE PATROLS' ONE believed-watch read (`believedWatch`, PR §8.3) and the landed belief reads — never on the foreign town's truth. A beast plans on nothing but hunger, season and cover. A town knows a body by what it has heard — ONE belief record per town and body, `believedBodyOf(worldState, townId, bodyId)` (believed size band · believed base · last heard where and what · the leader if known), built on read from the rumor content's typed `bodyId` slot (the MN7 idiom) and the sightings written into it (A.17: a site's `seenBy` of a body holder; a patrol's report; an informer) — the omniscient arm reads truth; a NAMELESS gang or flock has no belief record at all (BD23); a beast pack's record carries no name, only its place ("wolves in the Tolle hills") . The ENCOUNTER is truth (PR's `patrolEncounter`; the band contest).

**BD13 — EVERY ENGAGEMENT IS A HEARING IN ITS WEEK; A FLOW IS NOT A PARTY.** Every engagement of a body with another party at a place — two bodies meeting, a body raiding a settlement, a body at a held site, a body and a patrol, a body seizing a caravan that is a thing on the road, a body on an army's supply, a court's turn of stance — is a registered kind in its week (requirement 24; §12), with the engine's default and the DM's three choices; nothing queues over an advance; month, season and year advances run every week on the default. The aggregate FLOWS a body preys upon (shipments, migrants) are not parties: their loss is the landed roll's (§9.6), receipted and attributed, and the body's working of a stretch is ONE STANDING kind whose verdict acts on the body's coming week (`traffic_waylaid`, §12) — never pushed. Nothing toward the party: a body meeting the party is THE PARTY's kind, whose default is always "nothing has happened to the party yet". A CLOCKED engagement (a ransom race, a guild war, a sponsorship offer pending, a hunt taken, an alms request under negotiation) keeps ONE card on the docket EVERY week of its clock — a STANDING card with a weekly line and its current default — open to the DM at any week (leave · tilt · decide), closing at its AUTOMATIC resolution, which is its ONE CLIMAX week (the owner, 18:55: "the hearing is still avaialble to the DM until its automatically resolved"); the push budget counts it once . It is STANDING until its climax week and CLIMAX at the clock's end — requirement 24(b) ("the verdict forces the hand that week") binds that week — and the DM's EARLY act (pay · hunt · decree) CLOSES it early, in the week it is taken .

**BD14 — THE DEFENCE THRESHOLD READS WALLS AND WATCH, NEVER ARMY CAPACITY.**  A body may RAID a settlement only where its strength exceeds the settlement's ONE body-scale defence read by a margin: `bodyStrength(body) > bodyDefenceOf(T, bodyKind) × (1 + RAID_MARGIN)` (DRAFT 0.2, UNMEASURED as tuning, MEASURED as a matrix below). `bodyDefenceOf` reads WC §1.8.1's ONE brigand-vs-town read-point `defenseLedger(settlement)` (`domain/defenseLedger.js`: "the ONE read-point for the conserved defense quantities") — its `military` dimension (walls · garrison · watch · militia, "walls + garrison weighted highest") against a gang or a conclave, its `monster` dimension (walls · garrison · wardens — "defense against the wilds") against a beast — plus THE SITES' at-hand keep term on the site scale (`siteDefenseOf`; A.1's split), capped at 100; NEVER `homeDefense` (army capacity) and never a tier rule. The owner's line — town and above safe "because of defense and a walls and such", the rural band prey — is the WALLS term the urban band carries (`defenseGenerator.js`: "`if (inst.hasWalls) military += 30;`"), MEASURED on master's own generated settlements (`cure/D-BD-r5-probe/defence-matrix.out`, 72 seeded worlds' towns): at margin 0.2 a `modest` body raids 8/12 thorps · 6/12 hamlets · 2/12 villages and NO town, city or metropolis; a `capable` 9 · 8 · 4 · none; a `strong` 12 · 12 · 9 · none; a `formidable` (menace-scale) only the rare UNWALLED town. The read is a GENERATION SNAPSHOT (`fieldManifest.js`: "mode: 'snapshot', pulseWriter: null"), so its COLLAPSE is read LIVE on the institutions: while a settlement's walls stand `ruined` (`calamityKernel.js :: ruinInstitution`'s "`status: 'ruined'`" — a siege's or a menace's breach, a calamity, the DM's sack), `bodyDefenceOf` subtracts the walls term (military 30, monster 20, parity-pinned to the generator's lines — the landed "pinned equal" idiom of `ROUTE_TRANSIT_TUNING`) and the garrison's likewise while its institution stands ruined; then the town is PREY to the large (measured: a walls-ruined walled town falls to a `strong` body 1/11, a `formidable` 11/11) and scrutiny says why ("Kell's walls are down; the Tolle gang came in"). PREY is supplies and bodies (the owner, 19:3x: "they may terrorize thorpes, hamlets, and villages or whatever for supplies and bodies"): loot, food, recruits; a beast takes kills; migrants, caravans, and patrols at size bands. A CONCLAVE that is HOSTILE by stance (a court in its reach holds it `outlawed`, or `curbed` with its flock starving) MAY raid for supplies and bodies and MAY take captives (the owner, 18:54: "if they are taken by a gang or conclave"), its god's recorded axes the WEIGHT — an evil or warlike god's flock readily, a good one rarely and only in extremity (starving, hunted), never "never" (§4.2's conclave `raid`, the same choice word and the same act as a gang's; §9.2; §9.10); a PEACEABLE flock takes converts and alms, never by force. 

**BD15 — THE ENGINE NEVER SACKS, NEVER RAZES, NEVER TARGETS THE PARTY.** The worst a body's raid does by default is `succeeds` within its bounded take (§9.2); a sack is the DM's verdict only (MN8's law extended); the engine never razes or abandons a town. No body is ever moved toward, chosen against, or scored by the party (the hosting doctrine: "this does not compete with the campaign"); a body's choice set reads no party fact.

**BD16 — A BODY IS THE WORLD'S; A MENACE IS THE DM'S.** A menace is DM-named with a course and a target (MN1, MN12: "menaces are DM authored only, never organically emergent"); a body is engine-born from habitat. The DM may PROMOTE a body into a menace with ONE verb (`PROMOTE_BODY`, §11) that hands the body's place, size and name to MN's `RAISE_MENACE` and ends the body `raised` — MN's adoption idiom (the record of a driven raider stressor keeps its id, MN12). The only other road from a body into a menace is the menace's OWN authored recruitment (a gang that joins a recruiting menace by the menace's course, §9.8 (d)).

**BD17 — A GATE, NOT A CURB.** The conclave is the CARRIER of conversion: it gives the faith system's slow pressure a WHERE, a WHO and a SIZE a court can act on before a creed takes root. The court's stance gates ENTRY in both directions — a good flock in a good town is permitted from arrival and its god enters; a far god's flock is curbed or outlawed by default; the court's hearing overrides (§7.1). TWO STICKINESSES, measured (`cure/L-BD.md` §1(b)): entry into the ESTABLISHED set is share-driven and REVERSIBLE (standing derives from share; shares partition — a new creed's gain is another's loss; erosion pushes it back to `cult` or out) — the natural cap that stops conclave-driven entry from ratcheting; the PATRON seat is the sticky one by design (`PATRON_HOLD` 0.35 eroding 0.02 per contested week, the flip margin of 6 held 3 weeks, the patron contest and the unseating the only roads). The owner's "almost impossible to supplant them until they are replaced" is TRUE OF THE PATRON, not of every god in a town; this volume writes the distinction and cites WF. Curbing is not free: suppressing a creed that already has local followers raises their unrest and lowers the court's legitimacy among them (§7.5).

**BD18 — A TOWN KEEPS ITS OWN PEACE.** Prolonged trouble SPILLS by two landed carriers, never by copying a level: bodies MOVE (over the trails, across borders; a pressed gang, a ranging pack, an outlawed flock seeking a friendlier land) and HABITAT spills through the landed flows (trade losses on shared roads, refugees, a stretched watch) as a bounded fraction after a dwell (§10.2). Responsibility has three faces through landed channels: between PEERS a typed resentment patch and a grievance the neighbour may press; under a LIEGE a default on the member's obligations; and the INVERSE CREDIT for a town that keeps its peace. Order kept or lost moves the standing of WHOEVER HOLDS THE SWORD in that government — nobles in a feudal town (the owner, ~19:1x) — through the one legitimacy and power-share reads (§10.3).

**BD19 — THE MOUNT RULE.** Zero lines in `pulseKernel.js` (1581), `applyWorldPulse.js` (907), `warTermination.js` (818). The body stage is a LEAF at the head of `calamityKernel.js :: advanceCalamity` (461) after MN's, ST's and PR's leaves (+3 host lines, car-0 re-measures); every lean enters an existing seam as ONE bounded term in a leaf the volume names (§14); a host at or near the 800 ceiling (`factionCompetition.js` 775, `armyTransitKernel.js` 798, `institutionLifecycle.js` 800, `generosityKernel.js` 800) takes nothing but through a named leaf-first car; the frozen `roadsKernel.js` (838) is READ only — its T3 arm's fired outcome is attributed after the fact, never edited (§9.5) . Round 5's three hosts, measured (`cure/D-BD-r5-probe/efflines.out`): `routeNetworkConsumers.js` 184 takes ONE token (`body` in the frozen `HIDDEN_PATH_KINDS`, a DECLARED change on a landed access law, B.4); `activeConditions.js` 597 takes ONE template row (the guild war's condition, B.18); `generosityKernel.js` 800 — AT THE CEILING — takes at most an `export` keyword on `faithProximity` (+0 effective lines; else BD's leaf reads the same two landed axes with a parity pin, B.19); the round-4a guild-war rows in `stressorGates.js` are STRUCK (§6.8 (d)) .

**BD20 — THE PHYSICAL WORLD IS A SEAM; THE WAYS LIVE ON THE ONE LADDER.** Until the world canon lands, WHERE is node-keyed: a country around a settlement, a road stretch `stretch:<a|b>`, a site, or a hidden way `trail:<a|b>` beside a road (§5.2; the trail KEY names a corridor, never a rung — the rung is the ladder's, below); a card's WHERE stays on the fold's closed list (`road (a, b)` names the way on the card; a map-named JUNCTION is a key `junction:<id>` under `road (a, b)`, never a sixth kind). The host cannot invent geography: the CANDIDATE ways take THE SITES' two-layer shape — the map draws every plausible footpath at birth at rung `wild` (the contract: "marks them all 'wild'. Nobody knows them and nobody uses them yet. On open plain there are none"), and until it does the host DERIVES the same candidates deterministically from the digest's frozen terrain receipts. A way's STATUS is the host's ONE road ladder (master's `ROUTE_GRADES` `highway · road · track · hidden`, the contract's rungs `wild · way · trail · track · road · highway`, the host's `hidden` = the class {`wild`, `way`}): USE raises it (wild → way → trail; a town's adoption → track; growth → a chartered road), DISUSE lowers it (a road dies step by step, never erased — master's "A dead town's roads go HIDDEN"), a way nobody walks returns to the wild — the route lifecycle's own state machine and clock (WY; `routeLifecycleEnabled`), BD's only input the USE COUNTER its bodies' crossings add (the owner, 22:22: "routes are created and destroyed organically or drfit and that woul also regenerate or make new and hidden hiddne paths"). NEVER a second network, a second lifecycle or a second price. The candidate geometry is never stored; a lit way is the route ledger's own edge; what a town has LEARNED of a way is BD's (§2.3), separate from its status. 

**BD21 — BEHIND THE SCENES.** A body is shown when it ACTS — a Herald line, a dossier line, a map piece behind the bodies toggle (WY §3e's one builder, kind `body`) — and only once it is NAMED (BD23: a nameless body makes no line of its own; its acts read as the brigandage or the piety they feed) , and never pushed beyond the push budget (CHAIR-NOTES 2): STANDING cards are never pushed; CLIMAX cards are pushed only where the DM has pinned the country or the party stands near. On scrutiny the card shows the body's birthplace, its size, its supplies word, its base, its want's reasons, the stances held toward it, and the habitat and security of its country. The machinery (scores, weights, draws) is never on the surface.

**BD22 — EVERY CONSTANT IS DRAFT; THE RE-FEED IS SIGNED.** Every number in this volume is DRAFT, UNMEASURED, on the FP-46 roster (§17). Lighting the bodies' EFFECTS (`bodyEffectsEnabled`) re-feeds the embattled level, the criminal power and the raider gate from the bodies: a declared SAME-SEED SHIFT in every lit preset, the owner's to sign at its unit (THE PROMISE; OD-BD-2). The record alone (`bodiesEnabled` with effects dark) changes no other ledger (pinned, §16 B2).

**BD23 — NAMELESS UNTIL IT MATTERS.** Below its kind's NAMING BAND (`NAMING_BAND`, DRAFT `modest` for a gang and a conclave — the size at which it could raid a hamlet, BD14; UNMEASURED) a gang or a conclave is NAMELESS: it exists as a record and feeds the level, the quarter and the piety reads, but no town keeps a belief record of it, no Herald line is its own (the push budget's lever: nothing nameless makes a line), no court may post a bounty on it and no court takes a stance toward it beyond the default of its kind; it is "brigands on the Tolle road" or "a few believers in a barn". CROSSING the band it is NAMED from its place and kind by the voice program's pools (never a person's name — the engine names no person), its belief records open, its stance becomes a court's decision, and from that week a court may post a BOUNTY on it and take a HUNT against it by name; a leader may fill only a named body's slot. A named body that shrinks below the band keeps its name for a dwell (DRAFT 26 weeks — notoriety outlives size, RN's fade) and then lapses to nameless ("the Hollow Road gang is no more; brigands remain"). BEASTS stay nameless ("wolves in the hills") unless the DM raises a pack into a menace and names it (BD16) — but a pack's BELIEF record exists from its first sighting, carrying its PLACE and never a name, so the riders who hunt every beast (BD10) can predict where the wolves are . The naming is a RECEIPT, never a hearing. 

**BD24 — EVERY BODY LEARNS, AND BEING KNOWN IS THE PRICE.** HB's ONE habit law (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_HB.md`; the habit CURVE, `habit/habitCurve.js`, never the growth overlay — RULINGS-AFTER-RESEARCH §3) carries the bodies at three grains — the town and its court, its riders, and EACH BODY INDIVIDUALLY (never the kind) — on HB's one ledger (measured: a body id is an `actorId`; §4.4). BD mints no second learning law, no second decay and no second "known" price. Two learners meet in pursuit and evasion: the riders' prediction reads THIS body's habits, the body's planning reads the riders' cadence by belief; a habitual gang is caught and a habitual patrol is evaded; the seeded situation roll BOUNDS every habit weight (a habit never decides alone), the DM's `PATROL_ORDER` breaks a round's rut, and a new leader HALVES a gang's lessons (HB's seat shock at the body grain — `habitCurve.js :: seatAdjusted`: "a partial keep, never a reset", `SEAT_SHOCK_KEEP` 0.5 — the entry seat being the leader the row was learned under). An ENDED body's rows DROP through HB's ONE prune, which gains the actor-ended case (a body's `ended` ring row drops that actor's rows — ROUTE HB; HB's `pruneRows` drops only neutral stocks today, so without it a dead gang's lessons would ghost).  

**BD25 — A GANG HAS ONE END.** Every gang carries ONE end goal (`endGoal`, set at birth by its habitat and its leader's axes; the DM's to set): to DISPERSE rich, to FOUND A COMPANY OF SWORDS, to make a HAMLET of the site it held, or to build the GUILD its town's tier allows where none exists — and where a guild already rules the quarter it is SUBSERVIENT to it, unless it tries the overthrow that starts a GUILD WAR (§6.8). The company and the guild are founded far from where the gang is known (the owner, 18:46 — read as "where their reputation does NOT harm them", the chair's reading, the owner's to correct); the hamlet is made IN PLACE (18:47: "they have attained something like independence by holding the site for so long"). The engine never founds a champion-grade CORPS (DM-authored custom content, the owner's 06:32 word) and never makes an end an irregular's hire (the owner, 18:45). A gang's end is readable on scrutiny and never pushed. 

**BD26 — THE CLANDESTINE HAND IS A TIE, NEVER A STANCE.** A foreign court (or, for a conclave, its creed's mother temple abroad) may SPONSOR a gang or a conclave — never a beast — through ONE persisted tie (`sponsorships`, §10.4; THE OWNER'S shape): a seasonal FLOW by one of four CARRIERS, a PURPOSE that steers the body's choice only as a WEIGHT (the body keeps its mind), and TRUST both ways on RN's regard. The land-holder's stance toward the body stays the land-holder's (§7.1). The tie is CH's clandestine-hire idiom with ES-6a's leak — ONE exposure source (`covert_hire_exposed`), fired ONLY through CH's shared leaf `exposeCovertHire` (CH owns it; BD's stage calls it and never re-implements), ONE latch per tie (`exposedAt` on BD's own tie and stance row, written in the act that fires it; BD never writes CH's `paragonBeliefs`) ; a leak turns the blame, a casus on the landed grievance ladder and the hunt onto the sponsor. In peace it is the deniable weapon; in war it comes half into the open (the owner, 18:50: "cold war tactics both during war and in peace time"). 

**BD27 — THE ENGINE NEVER HARMS A CAPTIVE; A NAMED CAPTIVE'S CUSTODY WAITS ON ES-2b.** A raid's UNNAMED captives are a count on the body's size (recruits, labour), never people the engine tracks. A NAMED person a body takes (an envoy, a paragon — never the party, whose fate is the table's) is CUSTODY, which waits on ES-2b (the owner's slot); this volume writes the BODY's side of that slot — THE RANSOM RACE (§9.10), three words for three events: `ransom_granted` the body survived and the court PAID (at the clock's end or early), `captive_freed` the body was DEFEATED first, `captive_released` the clock ran out with no payment and no defeat (or the body could no longer feed him) . A captor is a gang, or a HOSTILE conclave whose god's axes allow it (B.2: an evil god's flock readily, a good one rarely and in extremity). The captive's fate past release is the DM's. The landed roads hold (`RansomRec`, the frozen gauntlet's T3 `hostage`) keeps its own landed economy for the roster envoys it takes; a body is only NAMED as its hand (§9.5). 

**BD28 — EVERY ACT DRIVES THE ONE CRISIS TRUTH THROUGH ITS OWN RECORDS.** MN4's law: a body drives the stressors and conditions the world already knows — the raider pressure, the criminal corridor, the religious fracture, the outbreak's spread, the site's own rows, the embattled gauge, the custody rows — keyed by its record; BD mints NO stressor kind, condition or crisis law, and where no stressor exists for an act its row is a named SLOT (§22, the volume's last section; one act driving two stressors is a double count unless the table says why). 

---

## §2 THE ONTOLOGY

### 2.1 The actor, the keys and the ref
- **The actor kind `body`.** A body DECIDES (its weekly choice is its own, §4), so by RULINGS-ROUND3 A.7's own test ("a corps decides … and is keyed `u:`") it is an actor: the convention gains a SIXTH kind, `{ kind: 'body', bodyKind: 'conclave' | 'gang' | 'beast', id: <bodyId> }` — ONE kind for all three, its kind a FIELD (as a person's archetype is), so no reader learns three new words; `POSTURE_ACTOR_KINDS = settlement · person · party · menace · corps · body` (OD-BD-3, RULED by RULINGS-ROUND3 §G.3 and RULINGS-ROUND4 A.12; the fold §1 / §3.3 and the spine mirror say six at P4) . The proposals queue's anchor `targetSaveId` stays a real settlement (the body's country's settlement); the body rides the `subject` dial (the MN convention). The `body` literal is a UI homonym on master (25 literals: card and HTML bodies) — registered; never a DM-visible word.
- **RN keys.** `g:<bodyId>` for a gang — a HOLDER (its feeling for the courts that hunt, harbour or employ it, written by the deeds heard at its home) and a SUBJECT (a town's commons may fear or love a gang); a conclave's key is the creed's EXISTING key `creed:<deityRef>` (RN §2.2: "a creed's faithful — a body"); a beast holds and is no key (BD10). The refused-pair rule's FIRST class (`s:`/`c:` × `s:`/`c:`) does not bind `g:` — a town's, a court's or a person's regard of a gang is RN's; its SECOND class does: body↔body feeling (`g:` × `g:`, `g:` × a conclave's `creed:`) is THIS volume's `pairs` (§7.2), and RN refuses it with reason `pair_state` . Three readers ride these keys : THE COMMONS' STANCE toward a body is RN's regard of the town's people (`s:<settlementId>`, RN §2.2: "the town's people, the commons") toward the body's key, banded `fear · indifference · support` (§7.6); a SPONSOR's and its client's TRUST is RN's regard both ways (`c:<sponsorCourt>` → `g:<bodyId>` / the creed's derived regard, RN7, → `c:<sponsorCourt>`; §10.4); a body's NOTORIETY is RN's fame of its key at a town (`fameIn(worldState, { kind: 'body', bodyKind, id }, townId, tick)`, RN §4.3) — only a NAMED body's acts are deeds (BD23). ROUTE RN.
- **ST's holder ref (RULINGS-ROUND4 A.3, CURED in ST at round 4a).** A body holding a site is ST's holder: `untied` with ref `{ kind: 'body', bodyKind: 'gang' | 'beast', id }` (a lair is a den held by a beast — no new kind); a CONCLAVE holds as ST's `faith` holder with ref `{ kind: 'body', bodyKind: 'conclave', id }` and `creed` its creed — a church's site, whose sign in each attached town follows that town's stance toward the creed (`courtStanceOf`, §7.1), never a general hazard; `cult` and `band` are never holder kinds (`cult` stays the faith ladder's standing) . An id-less authored gang ST placed before C17 becomes a body record at BD-0's adoption THROUGH ST'S WRITER (no holder change; its sighting rows handed to the body's belief record, §2.4 — ST's route (a)).

### 2.2 The record — `spatialLedgers.bodies` (persisted shape 1 of 3; NEW; THE OWNER'S, OD-BD-1)

```
spatialLedgers.bodies = {                                   // drop-when-empty; the spatialLedgers conditional idiom
  records: { [bodyId]: {
    bodyKind,                 // BODY_KINDS: 'conclave' | 'gang' | 'beast'  [r4: renamed from `kind` — A.12 · G.3: `kind` on a ref is the actor kind]
    name?,                    // the DM's words only, quoted, never parsed (MN2's law); absent ⇒ the DERIVED display name (§2.5 bodyName), shown only once NAMED (BD23)
    naming?: { since, lapseSince? },       // r4 (L-BD 32b): the tick it crossed NAMING_BAND; `lapseSince` the tick it fell below (it lapses after the dwell); absent ⇒ NAMELESS
    // r5 (B.12): NO stored leader — a NAMED body's leader is DERIVED, `bodyLeaderOf(worldState, bodyId)` = the paragon whose CH `paragon.leads.bodyId` names this body (CH's record is THE record); never a beast's
    endGoal?,                 // r4 (L-BD 42): GANG ONLY — GANG_END_GOALS: 'retire' | 'free_company' | 'hamlet' | 'guild' (one, set at birth; the DM's `BODY_GOAL`)  [r5: BD-r4-14 · BD-X7 — `'disperse'` is a landed literal, §2.6]
    subservientTo?,           // r4 (L-BD 42 (d)): GANG ONLY — the settlement whose criminal-power institution takes its share (§6.8)
    contest?: { kind: 'guild_war', townId, sinceTick },   // r4 (L-BD 42 (d)): a gang's open overthrow (the clocked `guild_war` card's state)
    held?: [ { personRef, sinceTick, clockWeeks } ],      // r4 (L-BD 34 · 34b): NAMED captives — DARK until ES-2b lands (the owner's custody slot); ≤ 2
    creedRef?,                // conclave: the deity ref (the creed's key)
    birth: { word, placeKey, fromId?, tick, causedBy },    // BODY_BIRTHS; the birthplace receipt (BD2)
    where: { kind: 'country' | 'road' | 'trail' | 'site', ids: [a] | [a, b], siteId? },
    homeId?,                  // gang: its tie town (the quarter it spilled from, its fence's town); conclave: the seat it came from
    baseSiteId?,              // the held site that is its base (ST's record names this body as holder; BD9)
    might,                    // STRENGTH_BAND_KEYS (MN-0's keys on armyStrength.js): thin · modest · capable · strong · formidable
    bodySupply01,             // the one supply stock (BD9)
    bodyCohesion01,           // cohesion (BD9)
    standings?: { [settlementId]: s01 },   // GANG ONLY: its grip per town (≤ 6 towns, DRAFT) — composed into the criminal power (§7.3)
    choice?: { word, tick, reasons },      // last week's choice and its ≤ 3 reasons (the receipt; the dwell's candidate)
    pinnedBy?: 'dm',          // the DM's hold: no weekly choice, no birth, no end but by the DM
    sinceTick, lastTick,
  } },
  pairs: { [pairKey(bodyA, bodyB)]: { label, sinceTick, incidents } },   // body↔body: label ∈ allied · neutral · rival · hostile; incidents ≤ 8 { word, tick, causedBy } (the pair-state idiom)
  courtStances: { [settlementId]: { [subjectKey]: { word, sinceTick, causedBy, covert?, exposedAt?, bounty? } } },   // subjectKey 'creed:<ref>' | 'g:<bodyId>'; a row exists ONLY where a hearing or the DM set a word other than the derived default (§7.1), or a BOUNTY stands (r4, L-BD 33: `bounty: { band, sinceTick }`, the purse band on the court's FUNDING_BANDS; a named body only)
  sponsorships: { [tieId]: { sponsorRef, bodyId, flow, purpose, carrier, sinceTick, lastDelivery?, exposedAt? } },   // r4 (L-BD 46 · 47): a declared SUB-MAP of this shape, the clandestine tie — THE OWNER'S (⭐ OD-BD-31); ≤ 2 per body; §10.4  [r5: RU-5 — `exposedAt` the ONE latch per tie (CH's idiom; `covert`/`exposedAt` on the stance row for an `employed` gang likewise), written by BD's writer in the act CH's `exposeCovertHire` fires]
  births: { [sourceKey]: tick },                 // the birth DWELL clocks, sourceKey = '<word>:<placeKey>' (§3.3)
  ended: [ { bodyId, bodyKind, name?, word, tick, causedBy } ],   // BODY_ENDS ring, newest first, cap 24 (requirement 23)  [r5: BD-r4-15 · BD-X7 — `kind` on a ref is the actor kind]
  seq,                                            // the mint counter (ids are FNV over birth word · placeKey · tick · seq; zero rng)
}
```
`bodyId = 'body:' + bodyKind + ':' + fnv1a32(word + '|' + placeKey + '|' + tick + '|' + seq)` (`kernel/proseHash.js :: fnv1a32`, the estate's one hash idiom; collision-checked against `records` and `ended`). ONE WRITER: `src/domain/bodies/bodyLedger.js` (normalizer, mint, every field). Requirement 23: `records` is bounded by the cap (§3.4) and the world cap; `pairs` drop when either body ends; `ended` 24; `births` keys drop after one dwell past their tick; `held` ≤ 2 per body and closes at its clock (§9.10); `sponsorships` ≤ 2 per body and ≤ 4 per sponsor (DRAFT), dropped when either end ends or the tie is ended (§10.4) . ONE LEADER RECORD : CH's `paragon.leads` (`{ bodyId, sinceTick }`, CH §7) is THE record; BD stores NO `leaderRef` — `bodyLeaderOf(worldState, bodyId)` is a READ of CH's field through CH's view `paragonOf`; BD's `BODY_LEADER` verb (§11) writes THROUGH CH's leaf `paragonRetainer.js :: setLeader` (a leader only on a NAMED gang or flock); a polarity turn, the leader's death or its resignation that drops `leads` drops the body's leader the same tick (no stale copy). The round-4a stored field is STRUCK.

### 2.3 What a town knows of the hidden ways — `spatialLedgers.hiddenWays` (persisted shape 2 of 3; NEW; THE OWNER'S, OD-BD-4)

```
spatialLedgers.hiddenWays = {                     // drop-when-empty
  known: { [settlementId]: { [trailKey]: { how, since, fresh: [stockInt, weekStamp], upTo?, watched? } } },
    // trailKey 'trail:<a|b>' — the CORRIDOR's key (a key, never a rung word: the rung is the road ladder's, BD20);
    // how ∈ TRAIL_LEARNED: by_catch · by_survey · by_guide · by_broker · by_pursuit · by_dm   [r5: + by_broker (L-BD 11g) · by_pursuit (11e)]
    // fresh: the knowledge's FRESHNESS on HB's ONE curve (11f); upTo: the node a PARTIAL knowledge reaches ("as far as the ford", 11e) — absent = the whole way;
    // watched: set by THE PATROLS' stage, through THIS writer, the week the court's round places riders on the way's junction road (PR's decision, §5.2)
}
```
A belief object (BD12), and KNOWLEDGE IS SEPARATE FROM STATUS (the map contract: "Knowledge is separate from status. A town only sees a path on its map once the life program says it knows it"): a way's RUNG (wild · way · trail …) is the road ladder's lived fact (BD20); what a town knows is this record, and a known way may since have risen to a track or fallen back to the wild — the freshness and the brokers' age are what keep that honest (11h). A town KNOWS a way — whole, or a PREFIX of its nodes — by SIX sources : its riders CAUGHT a body or a traveller on it (`by_catch`, written by THE PATROLS' encounter through this one writer; a CAUGHT operative gives up the ways it was given, ES's confession read deciding how many — `by_catch` too); an operative MAPPED it (`by_survey`, ES's product); a guide showed it (`by_guide` — custom content, the DM's); it BOUGHT it from an information BROKERAGE where the town or a reachable neighbour has one (`by_broker` — IN's brokerages, `informationBrokeragesEnabled`: the broker sells SPECIFIC ways from its own BELIEF stock, gathered from smugglers' tales, caught couriers, travellers and the commons, each entry with a FRESHNESS that is IN's telling age, PRICED by IN's brokerage model — never a second market; a STALE purchase is a dead end or a watched junction, and the broker's standing pays for a bad sale on IN's one reputation; BROKERS ARE NEUTRAL — the same stock sells a gang where the watch moved, the counterforce on a town that lets its secrets through a market; the owner, 22:20: "so no need to discover it in pursuit"); its riders GLEANED it IN PURSUIT (`by_pursuit` — a REGULAR unit, a patrol or a led detachment, never a caravan, chasing an enemy body learns the path it fled by NODE BY NODE: ONE seeded roll per pursuit week on the stream `bodies:glean:<unitKey>:<trailKey>:<tick>` — THE PATROLS' gleaning roll, PR's text, BD's write — weighted by the pursued's SIGN (its might band; HASTE — a fleeing body leaves more), the GROUND (the segment's cover: forest and wetland hide a trail, mountain passes show it), the SEASON (snow shows tracks, high summer hides them — the one seasonal read), the pursuer's CRAFT (a warden-archetype leader reads sign a noble's levy cannot), HOME GROUND (a patrol on its own country, PR's `lived` placement; a kingdom's riders on a member's land count as home only under a strong hold, THE BANNER), the pursuit's LENGTH (weeks accumulate the prefix), a CAPTIVE or an INFORMER (§7.6), the pursuer's HABITS; each weight DRAFT, UNMEASURED; the receipt names what was learned and why — "Kell's riders learned the drover's way to the ford: snow on the ground, a gang in haste"); or the DM said so (`by_dm`; the party's own discoveries are the DM's to tell the world). A known way may be WATCHED: `watched: true` is written by THE PATROLS' stage through this writer the week the court's round PLACES riders on the way's junction road (the round's own decision, its first-priority term, PR §2.2), and dropped the week no rider stood there for a season (PR's placement `lived` rows are the fact). KNOWLEDGE FADES ON HB's ONE CURVE (11f; the owner, 22:19: "That knowledge is retained for a while but is eventually lost tothe settlement at large"): `fresh` is a stock on `habit/habitCurve.js`'s curve — CREDITED UP by every refresh (a catch, a survey, a purchase, a gleaned node, and each week PR's placement `lived` rows show the court's riders on the way's junction road — USE or WATCHING), DECAYED toward neutral by the curve's own decay between refreshes (`applyCredit` decays the stored stock to now; the town's law word its half-life band, HB's) — and when the decayed stock reads within DRAFT one tenth of neutral the knowledge LAPSES (the row drops; requirement 23's horizon): the way goes UNKNOWN again and its coverage returns to ZERO — the slow cycle learn · watch · forget · lose, legible on scrutiny. NO second decay law (the round-4a fixed 52 weeks is STRUCK). The derived candidate geometry is never stored (BD20). ONE WRITER: `src/domain/bodies/hiddenWays.js`. 
- **THE KNOWN SET IS A UNION, ONE READ (RULINGS-ROUND5 B.13).** `knownTrailsOf(worldState, snapshot, settlementId, tick) → Map<trailKey, { how, upTo?, watched }>` = the town's STORED learned set above ∪ its OFFICE'S WAYS: every way within the jurisdiction of the town's PUBLIC-SECURITY OFFICE (§9.11) WHILE that office is HELD by a person of THE WARDEN ARCHETYPE — for a paragon, CH's ONE predicate `isWardenParagon` (CH §2.2: "`domain === 'wildland'` on the champion side of its LIVING polarity"); for a non-paragon office-holder, its landed ROSTER ROLE `warden` (`generators/factionRoles.js`: "`warden: 'watch'`") — never the display noun (CH16), and NEVER a warden merely RETAINED by the court who does not hold the office (CH's widening "its job is its retainer" is STRUCK by B.13)  — known AND watched by the office's trade (`how: 'by_office'`, DERIVED, never stored, and never fading while the office is so held: a new warden knows them the week he takes the post; a noble or a militia master without the trade knows only what the town has LEARNED). The jurisdiction is the home's land; for a crown's office under THE BANNER, the kingdom's ways by the crown's reach (RULINGS-ROUND3 §F; PL's `crownReachAt`). `TRAIL_LEARNED` gains the derived word `by_office` (a read's source tag, never a stored `how`).
- **Its readers, each reading the union and nothing else:** THE PATROLS' `trailWatchOf` (the HOLDER's coverage on a way, PR §2.6 — zero on a way the power does not know or watch), PR's `predictedPlaceOf` (an unknown way is never predicted, PR §5.5), THE PASSAGE's `hiddenWayFor` (the MOVER's own knowledge of a way for a small unit's quiet crossing, PS8 · PS §5.6), CH's routing of a paragon (CH §5's trail arm), ES's ROUTING of an operative (11c: an operative is routed on the ways its SENDING court knows — belief, never the ways' truth: a way the court believes and that has since been watched is the catch; the routing instruction is part of the mission's cover and cost — a careful court sends by the quiet way, slower; ROUTE ES), a VILLAIN paragon's own route (11d: by its own knowledge and belief like any body — a villain LEADING a gang knows the gang's ways, a villain HIRED by a court is sent by its employer's known ways; the same choice roll as a body's, BD11; ROUTE CH), a WARTIME UNIT's risk read (11i: only ways its court KNOWS; ROUTE WC/PS), the body's route choice (§5.3, through what it BELIEVES a town knows), and the map's way layer behind the toggle (the map contract: a town "only sees a path on its map once the life program says it knows it"; office knowledge counts as known). A catch on a way (`by_catch`) is written by THE PATROLS' encounter THROUGH this one writer. 
- **A body knows its own ground.** A body knows every way within its operating radius (§5.4; the country it lives in, DERIVED, never stored) and any way it has walked; a body new to a country knows only the ways it came by — its first weeks there are on the roads or the ways it can see (a way's candidate at `wild` is walkable by anyone in the hidden franchise; knowing it is what makes it CHOSEN, §5.3) .
- **The party's knowledge is not the towns'.** The DM may REVEAL a way TO THE PARTY (`TRAIL_KNOWN` with its `party` dial, §11) — a mark on THE PARTY's own record through PT's door (`party_made_known_trail`; PT's shape and writer), read by nothing in the world; the party's own discoveries reach a town only when the DM tells the world (`by_dm`); the party's purchase from a broker is the DM's reveal (11g).

### 2.4 The rumor slots (persisted shape 3 of 3; a declared ADDITION to the landed rumor content; OD-BD-5)
Rumor content gains typed `bodyId` and `bodyKind` slots (MN-17's idiom for `menaceId`/`menaceName`): a body's news is carried by its WITNESS settlements and never by placing its id where a settlement's belongs (the fake-settlement failure; pinned). A court's picture of a body is DERIVED ON READ (`believedBodies`, §2.5). The body's display name is derived (never a stored string unless the DM named it), so the slot carries the id and the kind only. 
- **ONE belief record per town and body: `believedBodyOf(worldState, townId, bodyId) → { known, mightBand?, baseId?, lastWhereId?, lastWhat?, lastTick?, leaderRef?, source }`** — DERIVED ON READ from the town's rumor records carrying the body's id (RN1's tale idiom: the body is the DOER of a told act) and from the SIGHTINGS registered as rows of the same source: (i) a site's `seenBy` entry where the holder is a body (RULINGS-ROUND4 A.17: "for a BODY holder it goes into BD's belief record … never a live truth read, never a second belief" — ST §2.4's `believedDenOf` reads the might band FROM here for a body holder, ST's route (a)), (ii) a patrol's `patrol_report` (PR §9.3), (iii) an INFORMER's word where the commons fear the body (§7.6), (iv) a caught operative's confession of a sponsor (§10.4). `believedBodies(worldState, observerId)` (§2.5) is the list form of the same read. The omniscient arm reads the truth inside the town's land. At BD-0's adoption an id-less authored gang's sighting rows are handed to the new record's id (ST's route (a)).
- **A NAMELESS gang or flock has NO belief record (BD23; a beast pack's record is unnamed, by place):** the word step writes no `bodyId` slot for it; its acts reach towns as the brigandage or the piety they feed (the landed news of a raid, a loss, a sermon) with no doer of their own. The slot opens the week it is NAMED.

| the new kinds | `bodies_meet` (`passed · parleyed · fought · merged`) · `body_stance` (the subject kind's `COURT_STANCES` line) · `traffic_waylaid` (`waylaid · let_pass`) | FREE (`bodies_meet` 0 · 0 · 0; `traffic_waylaid` 0; `let_pass` 0);  |
| the incident | `peace_unkept` (with a `bodyKind` field) · `peace_kept` · [r5] `shared_faith` (the near flock's bounded trust lean, §10.2 (a); FREE — 0 · 0 · 0, `census-r5.out`; B.19) |  FREE on master (0 · 0); the SAME word and meaning as PL's round-4 REQUESTED-family row `peace_unkept` (a member's land exporting trouble; an obligation by meaning — PL §4.8 (1); r5) — two records of one fact-family, registered as an aligned pair (the pair's incident · the fealty row) |
| `BODY_ENDS` (widened) [r4] | + `dispersed` (a gang's end (a): scattered rich into the towns) · `ranged_away` (a beast's: no country within its reach holds its habitat for a season); `dissolved` gains `into: 'row' \| 'institution' \| 'settlement'` | `dispersed` 0 quoted (POP-1's designed `column_at_the_wall` verdict, aligned: a group breaks up); `ranged_away` FREE.  |
| `GANG_END_GOALS` [r4, L-BD 42] | `retire · free_company · hamlet · guild` | `retire` quoted in ONE test file only (`tests/lint/goldenFreeze.walker.test.js`: "`['enroll', 're-record', 'retire']`" — the golden-freeze ACTIONS, another surface; registered) ; `free_company` FREE (the catalog's "Free company hall"); `hamlet` and `guild` ALIGNED landed words (the settlement tier; the guild institution)  |
| `COMMONS_STANCES` [r4, L-BD 43] | `fear · indifference · support` | `fear` a landed NPC CORRUPTION VECTOR literal (`paradigmAxisCatalog.js`: "`corruptionVector: 'fear'`" — the NPC card's surface), registered; `support` an account-nav and hireling-tag literal (UI), registered; `indifference` 0 quoted. INT's commons voice has no stance words (its `RUNG_WORD` is the grievance ladder `petition · gathering · riot`); `anticipatedReactions.js :: REACTION_BANDS` (`supportive · indifferent · wary · opposed`) is a COURT's forecast of a power — another meaning, not reused |
| `CARRIER_KINDS` [r4, L-BD 47] | `operative · smuggler · cache_drop · temple_alms` | `operative` ALIGNED (ES's operative); `smuggler` ALIGNED (a role label, `npcAgency.js`); `cache_drop` · `temple_alms` FREE  |
| `SPONSOR_PURPOSES` [r4, L-BD 46] | `bleed_roads · spread_faith · hold_border` | `spread_faith` ALIGNED — `settlementPolitics.js`'s landed motive key ("`spread_faith: 'doctrine'`", a court's aim to spread its faith, the same meaning); the other two FREE |
| `NAMING_BAND` [r4, L-BD 32b] | per kind: gang `modest` · conclave `modest` · beast none (DRAFT, UNMEASURED) | the might words (MN-0's `STRENGTH_BAND_KEYS`) |
| the round-4 kinds' words [r4] | `alms_requested` (`given · refused`) · `body_bounty` (`posted · withheld`) · `hunt_taken` (`undertaken · stood_down`; closings — the body's `snuffed_out · driven_off · escaped`, the unit's `beaten · returned`) · `guild_war` (closings `guild_prevails · gang_prevails · both_fall`) · `sponsorship_offered` (`accepted · refused`) · `sponsor_exposed` (`demand_made · casus_taken · let_lie`) · the `ransom` row's race closings `ransom_granted · captive_freed · captive_released` | FREE: every kind name and `undertaken · stood_down · snuffed_out · guild_prevails · gang_prevails · both_fall · demand_made · casus_taken · let_lie · ransom_granted · captive_freed · captive_released` (0 · 0 · 0, `cure/D-BD-r4-probe/census-r4.out`); ALIGNED: `given`, `refused` (the conveyance row's verdict, ST), `posted`, `withheld` (PL's recognition verdict `recognized · withheld`, "held back" — the same sense), `accepted` (`relationshipState.js`'s `peaceDecision === 'accepted' \|\| … 'refused'`, an offer's answer — the same pair), `driven_off` (PR's `patrol_engages` and ST's `site_taken` verdict — the same meaning), `escaped` (quoted in 4 TEST files — ES's custody beat, another surface; 0 in `src`; registered ) · `beaten` (0 quoted, prose only), `returned` (`houseLedger.js`'s house note `'returned'` — another surface, registered).  |

### 2.7 The stage — `src/domain/bodies/bodyStage.js :: advanceBodies`
A LEAF called at the head of `calamityKernel.js :: advanceCalamity` right AFTER THE PATROLS' stage (which follows ST's, which follows MN's; OD-MN-20, ST §6.1, PR §4.1), before calamity's own flag gate; +3 host lines (the call and the merge of `{ worldState, newsEntries, receipts }`); zero kernel lines. Bodies move AFTER the riders are placed, so the week's encounter reads this week's watch (truth) while each body planned on what its ties believed (BD12). Each tick, in this fixed order, every step writing only through `bodyLedger.js` (and through each OTHER owner's one writer where it says so):
1. **Ends from facts** — a body below its existence floor ends `starved` (beast, gang) or `scattered` (gang; and a moving CONCLAVE at zero supply — its people drift to the towns, §6.4 ) or `dissolved` (conclave, by entry); a gang whose END GOAL is reached ends `dispersed` or `dissolved` into its institution or hamlet (§6.8); a beast with no habitat in reach for a season ends `ranged_away` (§6.9); a gang that chose to join an ENLISTING menace ends `merged`, REQUESTING the absorption through MN's one writer `absorbBody` (§9.8 (d)) ; an end the DM ordered applies; each receipted with `causedBy` .
2. **Upkeep and the season** — every body pays its upkeep from `bodySupply01` by size, × the ONE seasonal need factor for all three kinds (winter raises a gang's and a flock's need as it raises a pack's hunger, §6.1); a subservient gang pays its guild's share (§6.8); cohesion decays (slow with a base, fast without; a leader's fall lowers it, §6.4); beasts breed in spring if provisioned, starve if not (§6.3); the season's SPONSORSHIP DELIVERY is drawn once per tie (§10.4) .
3. **Births and names** — for each country with habitat over security and room under the cap, each of the five sources tested at its dwell (§3); a body crossing its NAMING BAND is named, one falling below it starts its lapse (BD23, receipts only); a gang over its radius or a pack past its range is tested for FISSION (§6.7) .
4. **The weekly choice** — each unpinned body draws ONE choice from its situation-scored set on the stream `bodies:<bodyId>:<tick>` (§4).
5. **Movement** — by the one physics on the ONE road ladder, road or way CHOSEN per move by `bodyRouteChoice` (BD11; stance a prior weight, never the rule), within the radius (§5); each crossing of a way adds ONE to the route lifecycle's use counter for that corridor (BD20; the lifecycle's own write) .
6. **Acts and engagements** — each act either resolves as a receipt (a flow's loss, a recruitment) or mints its registry kind for the week (§9, §12); a flock's ALMS requests to the towns in its radius (§6.6), the courts' BOUNTY and HUNT forks (§9.9, §9.11), a guild war's weekly line (§6.8), a pending sponsorship offer (§10.4) and a ransom race's clock (§9.10) each keep their CLOCKED card for the week (BD13); the hearings registered with SP-9 resolve docket-first at the NEXT advance (BD hosts no docket pass) .
7. **Standings, stances, pairs and lessons** — the gang standings, the court stance rows (and their bounties) and the body pairs move by the week's resolved acts (one writer); each body's and each court's LESSONS are credited through HB's one writer (§4.4) where `habitConditioningEnabled` is lit; the commons' regard moves only through RN's door from the NAMED body's deeds (§7.6) .
8. **The word** — Herald and rumor entries with the typed slots; the map projection refreshes.

## §3 WHERE BODIES COME FROM — HABITAT, THE FIVE BIRTHS, THE DWELL AND THE CAP (BD-1; the owner's ~18:3x "do they just magically appear, or do they appear from settlements or dens?", ~18:5x "does one jsut pop up from a village or somewhere where appropriate scaling", ~19:1x "Gangs can also form from some immigrant goups or detached from them")

### 3.1 Habitat minus security — the balance
Each week, per settlement country T and per kind, `bodyBalance(T, kind) = bodyHabitat(T, kind) − bodySecurity(T)` (§2.5; every term a landed read, every weight DRAFT, UNMEASURED). A birth is POSSIBLE only where the balance is above the kind's birth floor (DRAFT 0.35) and the country has room under the cap (§3.4); it is DRAWN, never certain (the keyed draw `bodies:birth:<word>:<placeKey>:<tick>`, odds `BIRTH_BASE × net`, DRAFT), so the prospect is unpredictable and the scrutiny line legible ("the quarter breeds: hungry, the watch thin, the guild strong"). The level FEEDS BACK as habitat (a gang recruits where the roads are already lost) and is bounded by the cap, the dwell and the supplies — never a runaway (requirement 2: the counterforce names itself — the watch, the riders, the keep, the trails learned, the bread).

### 3.2 The five births (each from a landed fact; each a receipt; each sized to its source)

| Birth | Kind | The source fact (a landed read, or a slot) | Size | Where it is born | The receipt |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) FROM A SETTLEMENT — `spilled` | gang | the town's criminal power (`criminalStrength01Of`, the stamp) × unrest × famine (the crime pressure) over the gang birth floor, the dwell passed | by the town's tier: a hamlet's spill `thin`, a city quarter's `modest`, a metropolis's `capable` at most (DRAFT) | the nearest cover country to the town (§5.2), or the town's own quarter when no cover lies within a week (it works the town's roads) | "Out of Kell's quarter, a gang has taken to the hill roads." |
| (a) FROM A SETTLEMENT — `sent_forth` / `driven_out` | conclave | `sent_forth`: the MISSION — a creed ESTABLISHED in a neighbour N (share ≥ `STANDING_ESTABLISHED`) toward a town T where it is absent or a `cult`, odds ∝ N's share × the piety gap (T's local piety over the creed's standing there) × alignment affinity (`stanceOf(T's patron, the creed).cooperation − aggression`) × the road between them (`hopWeeks`), at a dwell; a CHURCH-seated N whose chooser picked `missionize` this season (`settlementStrategy.js`) births it directly ("sent out by Kell's temple"); `driven_out`: a court's `outlawed` turn (§7.1) on a creed's `cult` row, when its flock chooses `move` (§4.2) | the creed's flock: `thin`, `modest` from a city (DRAFT) | at its seat of origin, on the road toward its goal | "The Grey Lady's faithful went out from Kell toward Ashford." · "Driven out of Merewick, the Grey Lady's flock is on the road." |
| (b) FROM A DEN — `den_held` | gang (or beast) | an UNTIED holder of a site (ST §2.1's untied body ref — the `band` slot struck by A.3 — WC-12's ambient raiders' located form) IS a body: at BD-0's adoption every ID-LESS AUTHORED GANG ST placed before C17 becomes a gang record holding that site through ST's writer (no holder change; a menace holder stays MN's) ; a beast's lair BREEDS by season (`bred`, below) | the holder's might as ST read it (`might` was the DM's for an authored band) | at the site | "The band at the Tolle ruin is the Tolle hill gang." |
| (b) `bred` | beast | a provisioned beast at its lair in spring (§6.3) | one band below the parent (DRAFT) | its lair's country | "The wolves of the Tolle hills have whelped; there are more of them this spring." |
| (c) FROM THE WILD — `wild_born` | beast | the wild's habitat: `config.monsterThreat` (`plagued` > `frontier` > `heartland`, the landed `MONSTER_THREAT_TIERS`) × the country's COVER SHARE (§5.2) × the distance from towns (the wild is wide beside thorps and hamlets) × the season (spring births; winter drives existing packs to the roads, never births); the calamity bucket's beast wave stays the STRIKE (MN11: "A strike is not a presence") | `thin`; `modest` in `plagued` country (DRAFT) | the most-covered country of the region | "Wolves have come down into the Tolle hills." |
| (d) FROM DEFEAT — `deserted` | gang | DARK UNTIL WC-11/12 LAND (RULINGS-ROUND3 §G.4: the loser's deployment record and the lifted siege's receipt do not carry the defeat facts the birth needs, and reading them first would mint a second defeat read) — then WC's own `brigand` tag event on a `free_unit` (`peopleLedger.js :: TAG_EVENTS`), so the deserter gang IS that free unit in WC's `raiding` state, its counts conserved by WC's ledger; CH's corps failure rung `desertion` ("its strength band falls one, the shed blocks taking WC's own dissolution road") feeds the same road  | sized to the loss (the strength the loser shed, banded; DRAFT `thin`–`modest`) | the nearest cover to the rout | "Formed from the deserters of Kell's levy, a gang holds the Tolle hills." |
| (e) FROM MIGRANTS — `cast_out` | gang | a migrant flow that could not be placed (`demographicsMigration.js :: MIGRATION_REFUSALS` `unreachable` · `no_capacity`, the landed placement outcomes), REFUSED at the wall (POP-1's `column_at_the_wall` `refused` · `dispersed`, when it lands), turned away at a CLOSED gate for people (plague-R7's stance slot, CHAIR-NOTES 11 — when it lands), STRANDED by a slowed pass or a bad season (the climate band slot), or PREYED ON by a gang on the road (§9.6) | sized to the group (`thin`; `modest` from a great flight, DRAFT) | the nearest cover to where it was turned away | "Formed from the Tollish migrants turned away at Kell's gate." |

So CLOSING THE GATES IS NOT FREE (a counterforce on the people's gate stance, when that stance lands), and a group LET IN with no work swells the town's quarter instead — it raises the crime pressure the town's own spill reads (birth (a) by another road), never a second body. The migrant group itself is never an actor (a flow); the gang is.    

### 3.3 The dwell
Each source has its OWN dwell clock (`births['<word>:<placeKey>']`, DRAFT 13 weeks for (a) and (d), 26 for a mission, a season for (c) and `bred`): a source that bore a body bears no other until its dwell has passed; and NO birth in a country in the week a body there died (BD7: "never magically, never the same week"). The dwell is what makes killing a body buy a season of quiet.

### 3.4 The cap
ONE CAP per country: at most `BODY_CAP_PER_COUNTRY` bodies whose `where` or base lies in T's country (DRAFT 2; 3 in `plagued` country), and a world cap (DRAFT one body per two settlements, min 3) for cost; a birth that would exceed either is not drawn (receipted only on scrutiny: "the hills hold all the gangs they can feed"). The cap and the dwell are the volume's two runaway guards beside supplies (§6).  

### 3.5 Clock, tier, counterforce, felt
- **Clock:** weekly evaluation; dwell per source (§3.3); births are rare (DRAFT: in a typical canonized world of fifteen towns, a handful of bodies alive at a time, UNMEASURED — BD-1's experience read measures it).
- **Tier:** gangs and beasts SPATIAL CANON (they live on the land; embattlement is canon-only); a conclave's mission birth needs `faithSpreadEnabled` (the spread lane: dramatic and full) and the canon for the road (aspatially it hops the relationship graph at `UNPRICED_LEG_WEEKS`, CH's fallback); a `driven_out` flock is aspatial-safe (it leaves to the friendliest neighbour by graph).
- **Counterforce:** security (§2.5), the cap, the dwell; for missions the target court's stance (§7.1).
- **Receipt and felt sentence:** the birthplace receipt is the Herald line, named for WHO (the body), WHICH POWER (its source), WHAT EFFECT (where it now is); no forecast ("a gang has taken to the hill roads", never "a gang will raid").
- **Surface · scrutiny · machinery:** surface — one Herald line at birth (never pushed unless the country is pinned) and the map piece behind the toggle; scrutiny — the birth card (source, size, the habitat parts, the security parts); machinery — the draw and its odds.
