## §4 THE WEEKLY CHOICE — the house idiom: a bounded choice drawn by the seeded stream from a situation-scored set (BD-1, BD-3; the owner's ~18:1x "gather forces where it is feasible and RNG weighted rolls on situtation", ~18:5x "how conclaves react depend on their faith's alignment and the personality of their diety combined")

### 4.1 The idiom
For every unpinned body (and every creed's `cult` row, §4.2) each week: `bodyChoiceSet` scores each choice of its kind's closed set — `score(c) = BASE[bodyKind][c] × Π factor(c)`, each factor a BOUNDED multiplier `[0.25, 4]` with a REASON (the `{ mult, reason }` idiom of `STRESSOR_SPAWN_GATES`), every BASE and factor DRAFT, UNMEASURED; ONE pick by `weightedPick` on the stream `bodies:<bodyId>:<tick>` (the pulse's PRNG forked by a stable key — consumption-independent, so no other stream moves; the HBF row `bodyChoicePick`, BD-1's commit). The receipt keeps the pick and its top three reasons (`record.choice`) — legible on scrutiny, never a forecast on the surface. A CHAOTIC god's flock and a chaotic-led gang read the widest weights (the factors flattened toward 1: unpredictable in prospect, the owner's Dwarf Fortress standard). The choice DECIDES THE ACT; the act's writer is always the landed one (BD5). 

### 4.2 The three sets and what each reads

| Kind | Choice | What raises it (the factor's landed read; each with its reason) | What the act does (through whose writer) |
|---|---|---|---|
| conclave | `rally` | low cohesion; a court stance turning against it; a rival flock or a gang near (fear) | +cohesion; the creed's strength in its town this week ×(1 + ε) through `strengthByRef` (§7.4) |
| conclave | `preach` | high local piety; a fractured old faith; a court that `permitted` or `endowed` it; a peaceful (`peacelike`) or lawful god | the creed's strength lean at its town (the faith system's share law moves the share, `SHARE_STEP_MAX` 6); in a town where the creed is absent, `attemptEntry` at the faith system's own bar |
| conclave | `supplicate` | a LAWFUL god (`chaos01` low) — "petitions the court first" (the owner, item 13); a court stance `curbed`; a held shrine wanted | mints `body_stance` at the town's court (§12): the court's fork answers (§7.1) |
| conclave | `hide` | `outlawed` or `curbed` by a hunting court; a gang or an army that sacks near | in town: WF-5b's covert congregation (`.deities[ref].covert`, "in place, never a parallel ledger") when WF-5b is lit — else the row's ordinary life with the stance's strength lean; on the move: the hidden ways (§5.3); at a site: ST's `keptSecret` (court knowledge only) |
| conclave | `defy` | a CHAOTIC or `warlike` god; strong local followers under an `outlawed` stance | practises openly: the creed's strength rises (its followers rally) AND the court's legitimacy among them falls and their unrest rises (§7.5); the court's next `body_stance` fork reads it |
| conclave | `move` | `driven_out`; a mission's goal; the cap reached at home; a friendlier neighbour by believed stance | leaves (becomes or stays a body), or enters a town by `attemptEntry` and ends `dissolved` |
| conclave | `confront` [r5] | a GANG that terrorizes the flock's people (a `raid` `succeeds` or a `traffic_waylaid` on the towns or the stretches where its followers live, this season) and the god's `stanceOf(god, the villain's axes)` AGGRESSION (`AGG_GOOD_VS_EVIL`); a `warlike` temper raises it; the flock believes it can meet the gang at its size (else it shelters, `parleyed`) | SEEKS the meeting on §9.1's SEEK term (the owner, ~18:5x: "a good natured upstanding conclave … would more likley refuse, pursue, or fight a brigand that is terrorizing the people"): its stretch is the gang's last believed place; met, `bodies_meet` defaults `fought`  |
| conclave | `raid` [r5] | ONLY a HOSTILE flock (a court in its reach holds it `outlawed`, or `curbed` with its flock `starving`): `strained`/`starving` supplies; a rural settlement, a caravan or migrants within its radius it OUTMATCHES by BD14's read; weighted by its god's axes — an evil or warlike god's flock readily (`COOP_EVIL_TRANSACTIONAL`, the warlike temper), a good one only `starving` and hunted (a factor DRAFT ×0.25, never zero), a peaceable flock never | the registry's `raid` with the flock the raider (§9.2): supplies and bodies, a captive only where its god allows (§9.10)  |
| gang | `raid` | `strained` or `starving` supplies (a raid is a supply act first, BD9); a rural settlement, a caravan, migrants or a patrol within its radius it OUTMATCHES by the defence threshold (BD14) or the band contest's odds; a lawless home | the target's kind (§9.2 `raid`, §9.6, §9.3) |
| gang | `smuggle` | a gate town inside its radius where its standing or the guild is strong; trade through that gate | its standing at the gate town rises (§7.3) — the smuggling gate reads the composed guild through `buildSmuggleContext` (one smuggling law, EC/TR's) |
| gang | `bribe` | a watch whose wages go unpaid (THE PATROLS' item 16 slot: PR's riders unpaid, `wantSince` on a keep); a tolerant town (`effectiveToleranceOf` disorder high) | the court's stance toward it moves one step toward `tolerated` at that court's next `body_stance` fork (a court's act, never the gang's write) and its standing there rises |
| gang | `recruit` | `provisioned`; habitat (unrest, famine, the levy away, cast-out migrants near) | might up one band at the next upkeep if supplies hold (§6.2) |
| gang | `feud` | a `rival` or `hostile` gang inside its radius it believes it outmatches | seeks the meeting (§9.1) |
| gang | `move` | low cohesion and no base (seek an empty den: ST's `site_taken`); hunted where it stands; the radius dry of prey | base change (ST's statuses) or roaming |
| gang | `scatter` | `starving`, beaten, hunted with no trail it believes unknown | ends `scattered` this week (a receipt) — part of its standing remains as the town's floor's ordinary crime (no write: the composition simply loses its term) |
| beast | `prowl` | hunger (supplies below `provisioned`); kills inside its radius (livestock — EC's herds read; travellers; migrants; a patrol it outmatches); WINTER (hunger drives the pack to the roads) | kills: its supply rises; the target's harm through the landed reads (§9.6, §9.2) |
| beast | `roam` | the radius dry; hunting pressure; summer | shifts its territory (its lair stays its base unless it `flee`s) |
| beast | `lie_up` | `provisioned`; spring (breeding); wounds (a lost fight) | rests at its lair; `bred` in spring (§3.2) |
| beast | `flee` | hunted at a size it cannot meet; its lair cleared | leaves the region for the most-covered neighbour; its lair falls EMPTY (ST7: "empty = no effect"), a den a gang may take |

**THE GOD'S CHARACTER (the owner's item 13) enters every conclave factor ONCE through `stanceOf(the conclave's god, X)`** where X is the god or the actor in question: toward a GANG, X is the villain's axes (`alignmentAxis: 'evil'`, `lawAxis: 'chaotic'` — "a gang's 'alignment' is its DEEDS (villainous by nature)", the owner's §8 inherent value), so a GOOD god's flock reads `aggression` toward the gang (`AGG_GOOD_VS_EVIL` 0.55: refuse its extortion, warn the watch, shelter its victims, fight it at its size — the `confront` choice that SEEKS it, r5) and an EVIL god's flock reads `cooperation` (`COOP_EVIL_TRANSACTIONAL`: it extorts beside the gang, hires it, trades with it); the DERIVED temper (`deityTemper`) splits the good: `warlike` fights, `peacelike` shelters. A LAWFUL god weights `supplicate` first; a CHAOTIC one flattens every weight. 

### 4.3 Clock, tier, counterforce, felt
- **Clock:** weekly, one choice per body; the choice is a receipt, not a commitment (next week's choice reads next week's situation); `move` toward a base is sticky by cohesion (a body with a base weights staying).
- **Tier:** the gang and beast sets canon-only; the conclave's in-town choice aspatial-safe (it reads the religion row and the court stance only); every preset where its flag is lit; the OMNISCIENT PRESET's arm is the same (no belief inside the choice except `believedStanceOf`, which reads truth in the omniscient arm) .
- **Counterforce:** each choice's own price — a raid risks the band contest and a hunt; `defy` costs legitimacy among followers and invites `outlawed`; `bribe` costs supplies; `recruit` costs supplies; `scatter` ends the body.
- **Felt:** "The Grey Lady's faithful in Kell refused the hill gang's demand and sent word to the watch." · "The Tolle gang has fallen on Low Ford for its grain."
- **Surface · scrutiny · machinery:** surface — only the ACT's line (when an act happens); scrutiny — the choice and its three reasons on the body's card; machinery — the scores and the draw.

### 4.4 HABITS — one law, three grains, two learners (BD24; the owner, 18:30: "don't forget the habits formed by the settlement, its patrols, and actors in response to beasts, conclaves, and gangs and those latter groups (indiidual to the specific group/body) response as well to each other and the patrol in their pursuit and evasion.") 
- **The substrate, measured (no STOP; `cure/D-BD-r4.md` m4):** HB's ONE ledger `spatialLedgers.habits` — "`rows: { <actorId>: { <class>: { <action>: [stockInt, weekStamp] } } }`", its writer refusing only "`if (!actorId || !action || !isCircumstanceClass(className)) return;`", the class "an ARGUMENT", "`HABIT_ROWS_PER_ACTOR_CAP: 24`", HB's curve `habitCurve.js` (`applyCredit`; the decay and the tilt cap HB's), stamped on `calendar.elapsedWeeks` (HB's clock law), gated by `habitConditioningEnabled` (lit nowhere). BD writes NO second record: a body id IS an actor id there, and BD supplies only its closed CLASSIFIER and its ACTION KEYS.
- **(c) EACH BODY, INDIVIDUALLY** (never its kind; a beast too — it learns where the hunters ride): three of HB's twelve landed classes carry a body's lessons in their landed meanings — `route_menaced` (its way was threatened: the ACTION KEY is the stretch or trail it took, `stretch:<a|b>` / `trail:<a|b>`; credited UP when it slipped through, DOWN where it was MET — "avoid where caught"), `stores_thin` (its supplies were strained: the key is the target it struck or the town it asked — up where the take or the alms were good, "return where prey was easy, where alms were given"), `rival_ascendant` (another body pressed it: the key is that body's id — down where it lost, so it avoids what beat it, and a feud hardens where it won). The RIDERS' CADENCE is not a body row: it is THE PATROLS' host fact (PR's round, a cadence a body can learn), read by the body's PLANNING only through `believedWatch` (belief, never the roster — PR §8.3; ROUTE PR: the cadence as a learnable fact). The DRAFT credit sizes are HB's (UNMEASURED); a body's rows DROP when it ends — through HB's ONE prune, which gains the ACTOR-ENDED case (a body's `ended` ring row drops that actor's rows; ROUTE HB: `habitLedger.js :: pruneRows` drops only neutral stocks today, so without the case an ended gang's rows survive — the verifier's executed `hbGhost.out`) — and are HALVED when its LEADER changes (L-BD 39's "a new captain … resets a gang's habits" read on the landed SEAT SHOCK: `habitCurve.js :: seatAdjusted`, "a partial keep, never a reset", `SEAT_SHOCK_KEEP` 0.5, the leader the row was learned under as its entry seat — HB's seat rule at the body grain, cited honestly) .
- **(a) THE TOWN AND ITS COURT** (HB's court rows; ROUTE HB for the classifier rows): the court's stance PRIOR toward a body (§7.1's fork takes ONE habit term: a court that has curbed flocks drifts toward curbing the next), its GATE stance toward migrants (a town that bred a gang from refused migrants learns either way), and its BOUNTY posting (§9.9); the court's paragons as CH already gives them.
- **(b) THE PATROL** (PR's text; ROUTE PR): its ROUND is a habit (a predictable cadence — the map's cadence words made a host fact a body can learn), its grouping and pursuit too; the riders' PREDICTION reads THIS body's rows (PR §5.5: "THIS body's own habits where HB carries the body grain … else its kind's") — `BODY_HABITS` (§9.3) is the KIND's prior, the fallback when a body has no rows.
- **THE BALANCE IS THE PRICE:** a habitual gang is predictable and caught; a habitual patrol is predictable and evaded. VARIATION is the lever: the seeded situation roll BOUNDS every habit factor (`bodyHabitTilt` ∈ `[0.75, 1.33]`, DRAFT, UNMEASURED — a habit never decides alone); the DM's `PATROL_ORDER` breaks a round's rut; a new captain halves a gang's lessons (HB's seat shock, r5).
- **Clock · tier · felt:** weekly credit (HB's); `habitConditioningEnabled` ∧ `bodiesEnabled` (dark: every tilt is 1 — byte-identical); "The Hollow Road gang has learned Kell's riders come on the fourth week" (scrutiny) · "Kell's riders now ride the hill road first" (surface).

## §5 HOW BODIES MOVE — the one physics, the hidden ways, travel by stance, the radius (BD-5; the owner's ~18:3x "they don't travel the overt roads … maybe hidden roads that connect between roads, and multiple of them", ~19:3x "sites … acts as a home base where they maintain something of a bounded radius to in distance")

### 5.1 The one physics
A body moves on the ONE physics: WY's roads (the digest's primary hops), the season's cost (`seasonalPathCost`, SLOW NOT SEVER — winter slows a pass, never cuts it, HOST-r2-1), the climate band when it lands (the slot), priced by `distanceRead.js :: hopWeeks`. It NEVER asks passage (PS1). It is a node-keyed WHERE (BD20) that advances at most one hop per week (DRAFT; a body is slower than a column on the road and faster than nothing).

### 5.2 THE HIDDEN WAYS — the lowest rungs of the ONE road ladder (`hiddenWayEdges`, BD-5) 
- **ONE LADDER, MASTER'S RUNG.** Master's route ladder ALREADY holds the hidden rung, its access law, its price and its life — `routeNetworkLedger.js :: ROUTE_GRADES` "`['highway', 'road', 'track', 'hidden']`"; `routeNetworkConsumers.js :: HIDDEN_PATH_KINDS` "`['covert_envoy', 'smuggler', 'wanderer']`" behind `mayUseHiddenPaths`, which "fails CLOSED"; `routeNetworkConsumersTransit.js`'s "`hidden: 2`" "PINNED" equal to `npcConsequencesTuning.js`'s "`HIDDEN_PATH_SLOWDOWN: 2,`" ("wanderers may use hidden paths (slowly); armies may not"); `routeNetworkDecayLifecycle.js`'s "A dead town's roads go HIDDEN" (dark behind the virtual `routeLifecycleEnabled`; `cure/D-BD-r5.md` m3). THE BODIES' ways ARE that rung — in the map contract's words the rungs `wild · way` (the host's `hidden` CLASS) and, worn by use, `trail`, below `track · road · highway` — and NEVER a second network: (a) ACCESS — `HIDDEN_PATH_KINDS` gains ONE token, `body`, a DECLARED change on a landed access law at BD-5's unit (a spy is the landed `covert_envoy`, a smuggler the landed `smuggler`; nothing else is added); "armies may not" STANDS, widened ONLY for a unit within the capacity band in wartime by a DECLARED change at ITS unit (the owner's 22:51 word, L-BD 11i — WC's chooser and PS's quiet crossing own it; ROUTE WC · PS); (b) PRICE — one price per way: the landed `gradeLegMultiplier('hidden')` 2 (= `HIDDEN_PATH_SLOWDOWN` 2) for EVERY unit — persons, bodies, smugglers, spies, detachments, a small unit — EXCEPT BEASTS, ×1 (the owner, 22:19: "traveling on a hidden path for all units is slower, except for beasts"; cover is their ground); the season's overlay on both networks, slow-not-sever (a winter way slows, never cuts); `TRAIL_WEEKS_MULT` 1.5 is STRUCK everywhere (RULINGS-ROUND4 §E: "the LANDED constant wins"); a change to the factor is a cost-law version bump and the owner's tuning (THE PROMISE); (c) LIFE — the route lifecycle's own state machine moves each way's rung (BD20): USE raises it (the map contract: "Each use counts. After enough use the life program raises the path's status: wild, then a way, then a trail"), a town's ADOPTION to a track, growth to a chartered road between the way's two junctions (WY's adoption), DISUSE lowers it step by step and a road is never erased; a NEW way FORMS where bodies keep crossing the same cover corridor (a desire path: the candidate becomes usable after the lifecycle's use threshold, DRAFT, UNMEASURED); BD's ONE input is the USE COUNTER its bodies' crossings add (§2.7 step 5; a bounded lean, the lifecycle's own write). CONSEQUENCES by construction (never designed separately; 11f): a pack cannot be run down on a way — it is AMBUSHED at its den or at a junction; a gang on a way is exactly as slow as the riders behind it, so pursuit of a gang is a PREDICTION and HABITS game, never a race; a spy by the quiet way arrives later than one by the road. With `routeLifecycleEnabled` dark the ways are the CANDIDATES alone at the hidden price (no rung moves — the DEGRADED ARM; the map draws them `wild`). 
- **THE CANDIDATE GEOMETRY — derived, buildable now; the map's when it lands.** The map draws "every plausible footpath through the rough country, forest, hills, marsh, mountains, but marks them all 'wild' … On open plain there are none" (the contract); until it does, the host DERIVES the same candidates deterministically: for every primary hop `a|b` the digest carries a frozen terrain receipt (`routeReceipts[…].byTerrain`, "per primary hop"), its COVER SHARE `cover01 = Σ byTerrain[forest, wetland, mountain] / Σ byTerrain`, and (i) a corridor `trail:<a|b>` BESIDE the road where `cover01 ≥ TRAIL_COVER_FLOOR` (DRAFT 0.40); (ii) a BYPASS corridor `trail:<a|c>` BETWEEN DIFFERENT road nodes for every node `b` whose two neighbours `a` and `c` both reach it through cover at or above the floor — a way that skirts `b` (its gate, its toll, its riders), its cover the lesser of the two hops', its length their sum, its JUNCTIONS the `a`-side and `c`-side stretches — so a way CONNECTS BETWEEN ROADS and a rough corridor holds MORE THAN ONE way (the owner, ~18:3x: "hidden roads that connect between roads, and multiple of them") . NONE across open plain, desert, tundra or glacier (there a body must cross the overt road and be seen).  The candidate geometry is never stored; a lit way is the route ledger's own edge.
- **Capacity:** a way carries at most a `modest` body (DRAFT; "a body of thirty, never a column"); a `capable` or larger body is too many to hide and must take the road — so a gang that grows past the ways' capacity is forced into the open (a counterforce on recruitment). A SMALL military unit may take one too (PS's quiet crossing: slower, size-capped, witnessed only on a known and watched way — CARRIED at PS8 · PS §5.6 in round 4a; in WAR a unit weighs a way it KNOWS in its ONE risk read — knowledge, capacity (a column SPLITS into band-sized parts and pays in cohesion and time), speed and attrition, the GAIN (SURPRISE: out of a way the enemy does not know, the defender fights unmustered — a bounded lean on WC's contest's defender term; ESCAPE; PURSUIT at the way's pace), the RISK (a way the enemy knows and watches turns the ambush around at the junction) — WC's chooser and the generals design's leader weigh it, PS's crossing rule unchanged; L-BD 11i, ROUTE WC · PS · PR); a unit's size is read on the SAME might ladder as a body's (`thin · modest · capable · strong · formidable`, PS §2.1 · §5.6), so "a body of thirty, never a column" is one line for both . Caravans never take a way. A SMUGGLER does — the landed `smuggler` franchise (`HIDDEN_PATH_KINDS`): EC's gauntlet run moves onto a way when the smuggler CHOOSES it (the same choice roll as BD11 — the gate's stance `closed · taxed · restricted` and plague-R7's quarantine, the believed coverage on the road, against the way's time and capacity), is caught only on a KNOWN and WATCHED way (PR's trail arm), and a run that passes costs the holder its TOLL and the gate its stance's effect (the holder's own reason to learn the ways); a sponsor's courier (§10.4 (b)) is the same run with silver as the contraband; a smuggler is never a body (a flow or a named person on ES's spine), so no stance and no hearing of its own — the seizure is the gauntlet's own outcome, receipted on the way (the owner, 22:14: "smugglers can also travel hidden paths as well to avoid patrols and gates") .
- **Junctions:** where a way meets a road is a JUNCTION — "where ambushes happen and where patrols set their checkpoints. Junctions have names" (the contract); a corridor beside `a|b` meets its own stretch, a bypass meets the stretches at its two ends, and once the map names a junction its key is `junction:<id>` beside `stretch:<a|b>` under the card's WHERE `road (a, b)` (never a sixth WHERE kind; the fold registers it, SEAMS-r4-16). A gang SORTIES from the way onto the road (it stands on the road's half that week, where the riders may MEET it — PR §8.1's truth) and returns. The BELIEVED junctions (the ways a town KNOWS, §2.3) are the patrols' choke points: PR's round plans by them (ROUTE PR — PR cites, one read). By the owner's junction rule a chartered road may start or end at a junction on another road (WY's).
- **Belief:** a way is KNOWN or UNKNOWN per town (§2.3), wholly or as a prefix; a patrol's coverage on an UNKNOWN way is ZERO; on a known WATCHED way it is the round's coverage at the junctions (PR's one coverage read). A town learns a way by six sources (§2.3: a catch, a survey, a guide, a BROKER, a PURSUIT, the DM). "The watch has learned the smugglers' path" is the felt sign.
- **PLAGUE ON THE WAYS (a law; the owner's word of 18:27 on L-BD 37):** a gate CLOSED for quarantine (plague-R7's stance slot, CHAIR-NOTES 11 — the host's own verb, when built) stops the ROAD, never the HIDDEN WAY: a body that crosses from an afflicted country by a way carries the plague past the gate the town shut. A body is a TRAVELLER to THE PLAGUE's one truth — its arrival in a town's country from an afflicted one is ONE seeding through PG's own door (pg-1's column seeding into the epidemic ledger, counted against its per-tick `COLUMN_SEED_CAP`; PG: "Seeding lands in the epidemic ledger, never a second system") — never a second plague law, never a spread channel of BD's own; DARK until pg-1 lands (the slot); ROUTE PG (a bracket on the quarantine slot: "the road shut, not the way"). 
- **THE COVER IS ON THE DIGEST (L-BD 38's risk, settled by measurement):** the derived candidate geometry needs terrain BETWEEN road nodes, and the digest carries it per primary hop (`routeReceipts[…].byTerrain`, §0.1) — the hidden ways are BUILDABLE NOW on the host's derivation; the map's per-stretch ground classes (the host document's ENC-HP-09, "needs T2") sharpen it and the map's own hidden-ways layer (ENC-HL-12, MISSING; the ask `refs/PROMPT-FOR-THE-MAPMAKER-HIDDEN-WAYS-2026-10-18.md`) overrides it when it lands — the two-layer shape, the canon half first where it exists .
- **PIRATES ARE GANGS ON THE LANES (L-BD 45, the chair's one-liner, applied):** where the sea lanes are lit (TRADE M8's lanes; `seaRoadsEnabled` for the roads missions' sea arm), a gang whose base is a COVE — ST's `den` kind on a coast, no new site kind — works the LANES as a land gang works the roads: its presence row stands on the lane's hops (the one physics: lanes are roads in every read, CHAIR-NOTES 14), the lane's own losses fire through the landed piracy parity (`banditryLoss`/`scoreRoute` on lanes; the roads gauntlet's S3 piracy arm, frozen — attributed, never edited), and the patrols' and the navy's answers are the landed ones (W11's blockade is a navy's, never a gang's). No second piracy law. 
- **Beasts take both — the way at their own pace, the road as the easier ground** : a pack RANGES country to country over cover and its ways at ×1 (cover is its ground; the landed hidden price binds every other unit), and comes down onto the ROAD's half when the road is the easier ground — winter hunger above all ("winter brings the pack to the roads", §6.3) — where the riders may meet it (PR §8.1). Its choice is BD11's roll like any body's (hunger · the season · cover · the hunters it has learned, §4.4); a beast needs no access token beyond `body` (its kind is a field).
- **The map's canon overrides — THE CONTRACT (`refs/MAP-REPLY-HIDDEN-WAYS-2026-10-18.md`, adopted 22:52):** the map draws the land's every plausible footpath at birth at `wild`, names its junctions and draws each way at its current rung; the host sends the facts ("town A now knows this path", "this path is now a trail") through the window, never invents geography; the host's derivation is the fallback until the map's layer lands, the canon the authority (the site record's two-layer shape). The remaining asks (T2 per-stretch ground classes; the width/kind hint; the milestone; the size budget) carry to the next exchange (ROUTE FOLD — the map INBOX). Drawn on the map behind the bodies toggle ONLY once a town the audience may see KNOWS the way; the DM sees the ghosts of dead roads (the DM's tool) .

### 5.3 Road or way — the body chooses each move (BD11) 
Each week a moving body plans its next hop and CHOOSES the network for it — `bodyRouteChoice` (§2.5), ONE keyed draw on the stream `bodies:route:<bodyId>:<tick>` over the two (or more) priced options, road and each way it knows within capacity, `score(option) = BASE × Π factor` with every factor bounded `[0.25, 4]` and its reason (§4.1's idiom; DRAFT, UNMEASURED): (1) the STANCE PRIOR — what it BELIEVES the holder of each land thinks of it (`believedStanceOf`): `permitted · endowed · tolerated · employed · harboured` weight the road, `curbed · outlawed · hunted` the way — a weight, NEVER a prohibition; (2) BELIEVED COVERAGE on each — the road's from `believedWatch` (PR §8.3, "the same read ES/CH/PT use"), a way's ZERO where it believes the town does not know it and the round's where it believes the junction watched (a body that has heard a town's public-security office is held by a WARDEN believes that office's ways WATCHED — deterrence by belief, PR §5.5 (b) ); (3) CAPACITY — a way above its band is no option (§5.2); (4) COST — the way's landed ×2 (×1 for a beast) against the road's, the season on both; (5) URGENCY — a pursued body weights the faster escape, a starving one the shorter road to prey; (6) HABITS — `bodyHabitTilt` on `route_menaced` (§4.4: away from where it was caught); (7) WHAT THE WAY PASSES AROUND — an ARMY on the march on the road (PS12's enemy-column presence read as a danger weight), the territory holder's TOLL, the GATE's stance and its question (PS's ladder; plague-R7's quarantine) — the owner's "to avoid military and armies and tolls and gates". The receipt names the choice and its top reason ("the Hollow Road gang risked the Tolle road: the drover's way was watched"; "a tolerated flock took the goat path round Merewick's toll"). No option at all (no road it dares, no way it knows) → it waits, turns, or crosses the road at the least-watched believed half. A body that is HOSTILE by stance plans EVERY move to avoid patrols (the owner, ~19:0x: "hostile gangs, conclaves, and beasts are trying to avoid patrols") — the coverage weight carries it. A body sees ONE BANNER in a kingdom: the believed coverage composes the kingdom's riders by what the body has heard (RULINGS-ROUND3 F.4 — deterrence by belief, never the ownership). PR's encounter applies on EITHER network (coverage × cover; a way's coverage zero unless known, §5.2).

### 5.4 The base and the radius (BD9)
A body with a HELD SITE (ST's holder ref names it) operates within a BOUNDED RADIUS in weeks of travel from that site (`BODY_RADIUS_WEEKS[bodyKind][might]`, DRAFT: a `thin` gang two weeks, a `modest` three, a conclave one week from its shrine, a beast its territory of two weeks), priced by `hopWeeks` on the road and the ways it would choose (§5.3: the cheaper option it believes it can take; the ways at the landed ×2, a beast's at ×1) ; it raids, hunts, recruits and meets only inside it. MOVING THE BASE is the `move` choice (a new `site_taken` or an `abandoned`, ST's statuses; never a drift). A body WITHOUT a site is ROAMING: its radius is one week around where it stands and its cohesion decays fast (BD9) — the pressure to take an empty den. A beast's lair and its territory are the same shape.

### 5.5 Clock, tier, counterforce, felt
- **Clock:** a hop per week at most; a town's knowledge of a way FADES on HB's curve unless used, watched or refreshed (§2.3, L-BD 11f — the round-4a fixed 52 weeks is struck); a way's RUNG moves on the route lifecycle's own clock (BD20) .
- **Tier:** SPATIAL CANON (the digest's receipts); `hiddenWaysEnabled` for the ways (dark: every body takes the road and the radius is priced on roads alone — the DEGRADED ARM); `routeLifecycleEnabled` for a way's RUNG to move (dark: the candidates alone at the hidden price, no use counter written); aspatially a conclave hops the relationship graph (`UNPRICED_LEG_WEEKS`).
- **Counterforce:** the trails' capacity; known and watched junctions; the hunt (§9.3); the radius (a body far from its base is weak and short-lived).
- **Felt:** "The Tolle gang no longer rides the hill road; Kell's riders have learned the drovers' path and watch where it meets the road."
- **Surface · scrutiny · machinery:** surface — a known trail drawn behind the toggle; scrutiny — the body's radius and its route choice's reason ("they keep to the forest paths: Kell hunts them"); machinery — the graph and its costs.

## §6 SUPPLIES, SIZE, COHESION AND THE SEASONS — the counterforce that stops runaways (BD-1; the owner's ~18:1x "require reinforcements and supplies", ~19:3x "supplies also constitutes fight readiness, size, and ability to be. sites help them ot maintain cohesion", "Beasts simply need bodies for food and have to procreate to reproduce so beasts should mostly be seasonal")

### 6.1 The supply stock
- **Reads:** TRUTH — the week's resolved acts (a raid's take, a waylaid stretch's attributed losses, a beast's kills, a flock's alms from its followers' share and piety).
- **Writes:** `bodySupply01` (one writer, `bodyLedger.js`). Upkeep each week `BODY_UPKEEP[bodyKind][might] × SEASON_NEED[season]` (DRAFT; larger bodies eat more; ONE seasonal need read for all three kinds — winter raises a gang's and a flock's need as it raises a pack's hunger, L-BD 38 ); gains by kind: a GANG from its takes (a raid's `succeeds`, its attributed share of the stretch's fired losses, smuggling through a strong-standing gate, a bribe's return, an employer's pay when `employed`); a CONCLAVE from ALMS — its followers' share × local piety where it stands or preaches (a flock among the faithful is fed; a flock on the road lives on what it carries), the ALMS every town in its radius grants it (§6.6) and an `endowed` court's gift — and a HOSTILE flock (no alms; BD14) from its TAKES (its `raid`'s `succeeds`, §4.2) ; a BEAST from KILLS (§6.3). Every body that is not a beast may also be fed by a SPONSOR's seasonal flow (§10.4), a RANSOM paid (§9.10) or a forced SALE's price (§9.4); a subservient gang pays its GUILD's share (§6.8) .
- **Its three meanings (BD9):** READINESS `bodyReadiness01 = clamp01(bodySupply01 / BODY_NEED[bodyKind][might])`, worded `provisioned · strained · starving` (the war's own supply words, `warResolve.js :: supplyBand`); SIZE (§6.2); EXISTENCE (§6.4).
- **Counterforce:** guarding the caravans, hunting the raids, keeping the villages' grain behind walls — every one lowers the take, so "guard the caravans and the gang starves" is arithmetic, not prose.

### 6.2 Size — recruitment and shedding (reinforcements)
- **Reads:** `bodyHabitat` at the body's country (truth for the recruitment pool; the habitat is the world's), its ties (an allied gang's merge, §9.1; a sister flock's `merged`; a beast's spring), the trails' capacity (§5.2).
- **Writes:** `might` one band up when a `recruit` week (or a conclave's `preach` week among the faithful) finds habitat over the floor AND readiness `provisioned` for DRAFT 4 consecutive weeks; one band down when `starving` for DRAFT 4 weeks (it sheds), when it loses a fight (the band contest's loser), or when it splits on a `scatter`. A gang that outgrows the trails (`capable`+) must take the road (a counterforce). Reinforcement by ties: a `merged` meeting folds the smaller into the larger (§9.1).
- **Clock:** monthly-ish dwell (DRAFT 4 weeks), never more than one band a month.

### 6.3 The beasts' year (the owner's ~19:3x word)
A beast's supplies are KILLS: livestock on the land (the country's herds — EC's resource read where lit, else the tier's agrarian share; never a second yield law, ROUTE EC), travellers on the roads, migrants, a patrol it outmatches. By SEASON (the landed season read; the climate band when it lands, the slot): SPRING — a `provisioned` pack at its lair BREEDS (`bred`, §3.2) and new packs are `wild_born` in the cover; SUMMER–AUTUMN — it hunts its territory; WINTER — hunger drives it to the ROADS (its presence term ×1.2 on the stretches, DRAFT; "winter brings the pack to the roads", the climate consumer the owner accepted) and its upkeep rises; when the kills stop it STARVES and ends `starved`. So the beast population BREATHES with the year — a seasonal presence, mostly, never a standing one; a fed pack breeds next spring, a starved one shrinks and dies (receipted). No recruitment, no ties (BD10). A cleared lair is an EMPTY den (ST7: empty = no effect) a gang may take; a beast-free region's hunting and forage yield is EC's resource read (ROUTE EC; cite, never a second yield law). A settled region — POP's growth raising towns' tiers — shrinks the wild's habitat (fewer births); a frontier breeds more; hunting packs to nothing costs riders (PR's losses) and wins only until the next spring's `wild_born`.

### 6.4 Cohesion and existence
- **Cohesion:** `bodyCohesion01` decays each week by `COHESION_DECAY.based` (DRAFT 0.01) with a held base, `COHESION_DECAY.roaming` (DRAFT 0.06) without; `rally` (conclave) and a won fight raise it; a lost fight, a `starving` week and a dead leader's fall (a named person, the DM's) lower it. A body with a LEADER (§6.10) holds its cohesion ON the leader: while the leader stands it decays at the based rate wherever it is (a captain holds a band together), and the leader's LOSS — killed by the DM, taken into custody (ES-2b's slot), turned by a court's hire (CH's clandestine idiom), or removed by the DM's `BODY_LEADER` — drops it at once by DRAFT 0.3 and tests FISSION (§6.7): a band without its captain scatters or splits . Below DRAFT 0.2 a body may only `move` (to a site) or `scatter`.
- **Existence:** a body ends from a fact (BD2): `starved` (supplies at zero for DRAFT 3 weeks — gang, beast), `scattered` (cohesion below 0.1, or a fight lost at `thin`, or its own `scatter` choice — gang; and a moving CONCLAVE whose supplies stand at zero for DRAFT 3 weeks — its people drift to the towns, §6.9's SCATTERED ), `dissolved` (a conclave received into a town by `attemptEntry`, or one whose court turned `permitted` while it stood in that town — it is that town's row again), `merged` (absorbed in a meeting), `raised` (the DM's promotion, BD16), `disbanded` (the DM's end). Each end leaves its `ended` row (the ring) and a Herald line; its pairs drop; its site passes by ST's ONE writer in ST's own words — an ENDED body's site falls unheld, `cleared` (ST §6.1 step 1), a body that LEFT by its own `move` leaves it `abandoned`, and a body ended `raised` HANDS its site to the menace (the holder re-pointed to `{ kind: 'menace', id }`, never emptied, no unmaking telling, no reclaim — RULINGS-ROUND5 A.2) ; its HABIT rows drop through HB's actor-ended prune (§4.4, B.17); its standings stop composing (no write: §7.3 reads the live records).

### 6.5 Surface · scrutiny · machinery
Surface — only an act's line, and the map piece's condition word (`provisioned · strained · starving`) behind the toggle ("the Tolle gang, hungry"); scrutiny — the body's card: its size, supply word, cohesion word (DRAFT `holding · fraying · breaking`), its base and radius, the reasons of its last choice; machinery — the numbers.

---

### 6.6 THE ALMS — what a peaceable flock asks of the towns (the owner, ~19:4x: "conclaves that are not hostile actually request alms from all settlements how much they get is related to how distant their diety is from the settlement's patron and the prosperity of the settlement related to its tier and size") 
- **Who asks, and where:** a NON-HOSTILE conclave — one no court in its reach holds `outlawed` (a body hostile by stance asks nothing: it takes or starves, BD14) — asks alms of EVERY settlement within its operating radius (§5.4), ONCE PER SEASON per town (DRAFT; the push budget), a STANDING card in that town's week, CLOCKED while the town has not answered (BD13): `alms_requested` (§12). 
- **How much — ONE read, TWO factors:** `almsOf(worldState, snapshot, bodyId, settlementId) → { band, reasons }` = (i) the ALIGNMENT DISTANCE between the flock's god and the town's PATRON, read as the SAME `stanceOf(patron, the flock's god)` net that sets the welcome (§7.1 — one read, two consumers): near → generous, far → little, a town with no patron reads neutral; × (ii) the town's PROSPERITY RELATIVE TO ITS TIER AND SIZE through ST's ONE settlement band `settlementFundingBand` (its `prosperityRank01`, RULINGS-ROUND3 A.9 — never a second ladder): `unfunded` gives nothing, `meagre` little, `funded` a share, `lavish` freely. The band is a SUPPLY gain on the flock (`bodySupply01`, DRAFT steps UNMEASURED) and the DEFAULT of the card.
- **The pool is WF-7's slot (measured, `cure/D-BD-r4.md` m1):** the alms would draw on the purse the patron temple draws on, but "⛔ `templeWealth` HAS NO WRITER IN THE ENGINE (a WF-7 design-doc future …)" — so until WF-7 (and W-COIN for coin) lands no coin leaves the town; the alms are the flock's supply gain and a fork input, and the town's own clergy feel it through the COUNTERFORCE below. ROUTE WF (WF-7: the temple purse the alms draw on, one writer).
- **The counterforce — the patron's own clergy:** alms given to a FAR god's flock press the town's court toward curbing it: each season's alms at `a share` or more enter the court's `body_stance` fork (§7.1) as ONE input, weighted by the PATRON creed's share and legitimacy in the town (a strong patron faith minds the silver going to a rival — item 21's gate); a near god's flock's alms move nothing.
- **The DM's three choices** on `alms_requested`: leave (the read's band is given), tilt (one band more or less), decide (`given` · `refused`); a refusal ends the clock and the flock leans to `move` (§4.2).
- **Felt:** "The Grey Lady's faithful asked alms of Ashford; the town gave freely." (scrutiny line — never pushed) · "Merewick gave the Red Friar's flock nothing; its own priests would not hear of it."

### 6.7 FISSION — the split that balances the merge (L-BD 35, the owner's 18:27 word) 
JOIN existed (`merged`, §9.1) and SPLIT did not — a ratchet toward ever-larger bodies. One fission law per kind, each bounded by the cap per country (§3.4: a split that would exceed it does not happen — the larger simply sheds a band):
- **A gang SPLITS** when it is over its radius (its standings or its acts reach towns beyond `BODY_RADIUS_WEEKS` of its base — a grip it cannot hold) or when its cohesion falls below DRAFT 0.35 while it is `capable` or larger, or the week it loses its LEADER (§6.4): TWO records, the larger keeping the name, the base and the leader, the smaller born `spilled` from the parent (`birth.fromId` the parent's id; the receipt names the cause: "the Hollow Road gang has split; a lesser band holds the ford"), each at one band below the parent, supplies divided by size. A remnant below the naming band is NAMELESS (BD23).
- **A conclave never splits on its own.** A creed's row in a town SCHISMS only by the faith system's landed contest (`religionState.js :: resolvePatronContest` — "A SCHISM: a rival shares the patron's own niche"); a moving flock stays one flock (one creed, one people) and ends by entry, by scattering or by its creed's row's fate (§6.9). No BD schism law.
- **A pack SPLITS BY TERRITORY** when it outgrows its range: `strong` or larger and its radius holds no unhunted kill-country left for a season → two packs, the young one `bred` from the parent in the most-covered neighbouring country of the parent's range. A beast pack never merges with a stranger pack (`bodies_meet` between packs: `fought` or `passed`, BD10).
- **Clock · counterforce:** tested weekly in the births step (§2.7 step 3); the cap and the dwell bound it (a source that split bears no other until its dwell).

### 6.8 THE GANG'S END — one of four (BD25; the owner, 18:43 · 18:45 · 18:46 · 18:47) 
Every gang carries ONE `endGoal` (`GANG_END_GOALS`, §2.6), set at birth by its habitat and — once named and led — by its leader's axes (a greedy captain `retire`s, a soldierly one founds a `free_company`, a patient one makes a `hamlet`, an ambitious one builds the `guild`; DRAFT weights UNMEASURED), the DM's to set (`BODY_GOAL`, §11). It is readable on scrutiny ("the Hollow Road gang means to found a hall of swords") and never pushed.
- **(a) `retire` → DISPERSE.** When its supplies' COIN (the gang's `bodySupply01` above its need — the sustenance stock's surplus) passes a SATISFACTION band (DRAFT `bodySupply01 ≥ 0.9` for 8 weeks, UNMEASURED), the gang ends `dispersed`: its people go back to the towns where they are NOT KNOWN (the towns whose `believedBodyOf` holds no record of it, 42 (e)) — the receipt names where they went ("the Hollow Road gang has scattered, rich; its men are in Ashford and Low Ford now"). When POP's people ledger runs they rejoin those towns' population through POP's ledger (the spill-out birth in reverse; ROUTE POP); until then a receipt only, no census credit (macro over micro; the birth's own rule).
- **(b) `free_company` → A MERCENARY INSTITUTION (the owner, 18:45: "establishing a merecenary institution where approprate not become an irregular").** The gang founds the catalog's MERCENARY institution of the town's tier — `"Free company hall"` (town) or `"Mercenary quarter"` (city · metropolis) (`data/institutionalCatalog.js`; measured, `cure/D-BD-r4.md` m6: NOT a new institution kind) — in a town where the tier admits it and NONE exists, in a DIFFERENT REGION (below); the gang ends `dissolved` (`into: 'institution'`); the institution counts as POWER in that town (the owner's definition) and HIRES OUT to courts through the parked MERCENARY MARKET (WC (e), closed into CH by CHAIR-NOTES 16 — this is that market's BIRTH PATH: contracts as ties, the hired swords a unit in WC's union for the contract's war; ROUTE CH · WC). The founding goes through the ONE institution writer (`institutionLifecycle.js :: applyInstitutionLifecycleOutcome`, `fate: 'founded'`; the host is AT the 800 ceiling — BD's leaf calls the exported writer and adds NO host line; if a founding cause arm is needed a LEAF-FIRST car moves lines out first — BD-8's car-0, PLAUSIBLE). The champion-grade company — a CORPS paragon with a captain and fame — stays DM-AUTHORED custom content (the owner's 06:32 word); the engine never produces one. Being hired as IRREGULARS (`employed`, §9.8) stays a WARTIME TIE, never an end.
- **(c) `hamlet` → A SETTLEMENT FOUNDED IN PLACE (the owner, 18:47: "the hamlet is different, they have attained something like independence by holding the site for so long").** A gang that has HELD its base site long enough with funds enough (the hold's TENURE — the tick of the newest holder-change event in ST's site `events` ring, a `taken` or `placed` whose `by` is this body — older than DRAFT 156 weeks, and `bodySupply01 ≥ 0.75`, UNMEASURED; ST's record has a site-level `sinceTick` and an 8-row events ring but no holder-tenure field, so where the taking has been evicted from the ring the tenure reads AT LEAST the ring's oldest row — a lower bound that only understates a long hold; ROUTE ST if a holder-since field is wanted)  founds a HAMLET over that site, IN PLACE: born UNSWORN (no liege; PL's fealty question may be asked of it later; it may be claimed, besieged or sworn like any settlement), carrying the PAIR STATES the gang earned (hostile with the towns it raided, tolerated where it was tolerated — the landed relationship labels written onto the new settlement's pairs by the founding writer, one writer), its first ruling power the gang's leadership as an INSTITUTION (no named fate; the captain is a founder, never a character the engine names), the site persisting under it (the owner's sites-3 word, ST14); its people are the gang's and whoever the site drew. ⛔ MEASURED (`cure/D-BD-r4.md` m7): master has NO runtime settlement-founding writer — so end (c) is a SLOT, DARK until POP's runtime founding lands (a new settlement record at runtime is the OWNER's capability: ⭐ OD-BD-35 — STOP-BD-r4-1 RULED AS PROPOSED, RULINGS-ROUND4 §E ); until then a gang whose goal is `hamlet` holds its site and lives on (its goal unreached, readable on scrutiny). ROUTE POP.
- **(d) `guild` → THE GUILD, OR SUBSERVIENCE, OR THE GUILD WAR.** A gang may BUILD the criminal institution its town's TIER and SIZE admit — the catalog's own rules, measured (`data/institutionalCatalog.js`; `cure/D-BD-r5.md` BD-X5): the THIEVES' GUILD where the `criminalPower` exclusive group holds NO member in that place ("`exclusiveGroup: 'criminalPower'`" — a city's `"Thieves' guild chapter"` at 10,000 souls or more, a metropolis's `"Thieves' guild (powerful)"`; the group's other member, `"Multiple criminal factions"` (city · metropolis), also fills the slot, so where it stands no guild is founded and the gang is one more faction in that turf, its standing composing as any gang's, §7.3); the `"Assassins' guild"` (metropolis only, NO exclusive group) where none already stands; the TOWN tier holds no criminal-power member, so a town-born gang whose goal is the guild seeks a city or a metropolis (the different-region rule, (e), moves it)  — in a DIFFERENT REGION (below), through the same one institution writer; the gang ends `dissolved` (`into: 'institution'`) and the guild counts as power. Where a THIEVES' GUILD DOES exist, the gang is SUBSERVIENT to it (`subservientTo` the guild's town): a TIE — the guild takes a SHARE of every take (DRAFT one fifth, out of `bodySupply01`, UNMEASURED) and the gang is the guild's arm on the roads (its standing in that town composes with the guild's floor as any gang's does, §7.3) — UNLESS it tries the OVERTHROW: THE GUILD WAR (`contest: { kind: 'guild_war', townId, sinceTick }`), whose only interesting outcome the owner named ("it sparks a thieves guilds war which drastically increases crime and lowers internal security until one or both (the institution or the gang) are disposed of") — carried on the ONE channel both of the town's reads consume : while the war lasts the guild's town carries ONE ACTIVE CONDITION, `guild_war`, written through the landed condition writer `activeConditions.js :: withActiveCondition` (the kernel's own inline-condition idiom for `council_schism`) by BD's leaf, its archetype registered as ONE declared template row in `CONDITION_ARCHETYPE_TEMPLATES` at BD-8 (the one-row host edit, 597 eff), its `affectedSystems: ['criminal_opportunity', 'defense_readiness', 'social_trust']` — so the ONE affectedSystems join (`causalState.js :: applyConditions`: "THE single active-condition scan") RAISES the town's criminal opportunity (`deriveCriminalOpportunity`, mode `gain` — the crime pressure the gauge's `crime01` reads) and TAXES its defence readiness (`deriveDefenseReadiness` — the first of the three terms the defence pressure averages, which the kernel's `security01 = 1 − defence pressure` and BD's own `bodySecurity` read) — the town's internal security falls through the ONE security read, NEVER a write of BD's and never a second security law (BD8); its SEVERITY is the war's (DRAFT: `0.5` at the war's first week, rising one step a month to `0.8` while both stand, UNMEASURED), and the condition is LIFTED the week the war closes (its `triggeredAt` the war's opening act, its `causes` the gang and the guild); the commons' grievance rises as INT's commons voice reads the town's crime and order (no write of BD's). It runs until ONE OR BOTH are DISPOSED OF: the court's hunt (§9.11), the guild's own hand (each week one keyed band contest of the gang's `bodyStrength` against the guild's — the institution's criminal power read as a might, DRAFT), or exhaustion (supplies). A guild war is the CLOCKED STANDING card `guild_war` (§12) with a weekly line and its CLIMAX the week it resolves: `guild_prevails` (the gang `scattered`) · `gang_prevails` (the guild institution RUINED through `calamityKernel.js :: ruinInstitution` with the closed fate `captured_by_local_powers` — a member of the walker-closed `WORLD_PULSE_FATE_KIND`, no new fate  — and the gang `dissolved` into a new guild in its place) · `both_fall` (the guild ruined with the closed fate `disbanded`, and the gang scattered). 
- **(e) THE PLACE OF A FOUNDING (the owner, 18:46 — "in a different region than the one they terrified (they would have to travel) where their repuation does harm them", read as DOES NOT harm them, the chair's reading, the owner's to correct; corrected by the owner at 18:47 for the hamlet):** ends (b) and (d) happen in a DIFFERENT REGION from the one the gang terrorized — a town (or its country) where the gang's NOTORIETY as THAT town believes it (`believedBodyOf` · RN's `fameIn` of the gang's key, with its reach and fade — one reputation truth) is BELOW the band at which it would be hunted or refused (its stance by deeds, §7.1) and where the welcome by law and alignment is at least `tolerated`; this FORCES a base MOVE across country (§5.4's radius re-based; the journey on the ways or the roads by stance, exposed to the patrols and the other bodies, §5.3 · §9.1): RN's fade is what makes "going far to go straight" honest. A gang too notorious to be unknown anywhere has only (a) left. End (c) is in place (above).

### 6.9 THE OTHER KINDS' ENDS — the symmetries (L-BD 49 (a)) 
- **A conclave's ends are its creed's row's fates, in the faith system's own words:** ESTABLISHED (its share in the town reaches `STANDING_ESTABLISHED` — the creed is a temple institution there now, BD17; the flock no longer chooses weekly, BD3) · SUPPRESSED back into the patron's fold (its share eroded to nothing; the faith row's own latent `suppressed` state) · SCATTERED (a moving flock beaten, starved or curbed to nothing; its people drift to the towns) · and the flock DRIVEN OUT is not an end but the birth `driven_out` (§3.2). One word each, the faith system's where the faith system owns the fact.
- **A beast's ends:** `starved` (its kills stopped — the brief's "died out") · `ranged_away` (habitat lost to growing towns: no country in its reach holds its habitat for a season — POP's growth shrinking the wild) · `raised` (the DM named it a menace — the brief's "named", BD16).

### 6.10 NAMES AND LEADERS (BD23; L-BD 32 · 32b, the owner's 18:27 and 18:58 words) 
- **The name** is DERIVED from place and kind by the voice program's pools ("the Hollow Road gang" · "the Grey Lady's flock at Kell"), NEVER a person's name (the engine names no person) and NEVER the code word "conclave" (six landed name regexes would class it ARCANE — the compile's census hazard); a beast pack is DESCRIBED by its place ("wolves in the Tolle hills"), never named (BD23) ; shown only once the body is NAMED (`namingOf`, BD23); the DM's own `name` (quoted, never parsed) wins. ROUTE the voice program (the pools' corpus row — the owner's UI + prose release).
- **The leader slot:** a NAMED gang or conclave has ONE OPTIONAL leader, a PARAGON — DM-authored custom content through CH's retainer idiom, the field `paragon.leads` (CH §7: "a sibling field, not a `kind` on the retainer"; the villain who leads the gang; the prophet who leads the flock); CH's field is THE record and BD's `bodyLeaderOf` only READS it (B.12 — no stored `leaderRef`); `BODY_LEADER` writes through CH's `setLeader`; then the body's weights read the leader's axes (§4.1 (i)), its cohesion rides the leader (§6.4), and a new leader HALVES its lessons (§4.4, HB's seat shock). The paragon's leg MIRRORS the body's (CH §4.2 (3); CH-V3's law: two movers never share a record; the body's place is the leader's destination each week). A polarity turn, the leader's death (the DM's) or its resignation drops `leads` — and so the body's leader — at once. Bodies are FACELESS by default; beasts have no leader. 

## §7 STANDINGS AND STANCES — how the world meets a body, and how bodies meet each other (BD-2, BD-3; the owner's ~18:1x "gather ties amongst local settlements appropriate for their status", ~18:3x "antagonisti, neutral, or supportive of each other", ~18:5x "a conclave with a good natured diety might have welcome reception … ironically there may be gangs that recieve good purchase in evil chaotic settings")

### 7.1 Court ↔ body — the stance, its default, its hearing
- **Words (closed, per kind):** a court toward a conclave `permitted · curbed · endowed · outlawed`; toward a gang `tolerated · hunted · employed · harboured`; toward a beast always `hunt` (BD10). A row in `courtStances` exists ONLY where the court's hearing or the DM set a word other than the derived default.
- **The CONCLAVE default (`courtStanceOf`, the owner's alignment word):** `d = stanceOf(the town's PATRON god, the flock's god)`; `net = d.cooperation − d.aggression`, shifted DOWN by DRAFT 0.15 where the patron's legitimacy is below `LEGIT_ORGANIC_CONTEST` 0.3 ("a weak patron suppresses rivals harder") and UP by DRAFT 0.15 where the creed already has local followers (a share in the town); `net ≥ 0` → `permitted` (from arrival: NOT a public enemy, NOT pursued by patrols); `−0.3 ≤ net < 0` → `curbed`; `net < −0.3` → `outlawed` (DRAFT bands). A town with no patron god reads `permitted`. `endowed` is NEVER a default: it is the court's act (the hearing, the DM, or a church seat whose own creed it is — then the flock is the patron's, not a conclave). Truth in the default (a court knows its own mind); the BODY's knowledge of it is belief (§5.3).
- **The GANG default (the town's law and alignment, the owner's "good purchase in evil chaotic settings"):** from the town's EFFECTIVE TOLERANCE (`institutionTolerance.js :: effectiveToleranceOf` — "how much cruelty (+) or disorder (+) it will abide", its baseline the patron's conviction): `tol = (cruelty + disorder) / 2`; `tol ≥ 0.3` → `tolerated` (a chaotic or evil court — the owner's "ironically there may be gangs that recieve good purchase in evil chaotic settings"); OTHERWISE → `hunted` (the owner, ~18:3x: "These are public enemies to most") — a lawful-good court and a true-neutral one alike; a PATRON-LESS town (every deity-free world and every town without a patron: `institutionTolerance.js :: patronConviction` — "`if (!patron) return { cruelty: 0, disorder: 0 };`" — reads tolerance 0, which must not read as leave to ride) takes its LAW from its GOVERNMENT: `hunted`, except where its underworld rules (`criminal_shadow_rule`, the faction plane's government, §10.3) — then `tolerated` . `employed` is the court's act (its hearing default proposes it where `tol ≥ 0.5` and the court is at war or a rival it believes the gang preys on stands near — "how power works there"); `harboured` is DERIVED where a court that `tolerated`s a gang holds its base while the gang's LIEGE-or-neighbour court `hunted`s it. PL PRICES it ONLY where the LIEGE has ORDERED the hunt — a standing order read by PL's `huntOrderOf` (PL §4.8 (3), OD-PL-57): then a `fealty_defied` row with NO break (RULINGS-ROUND2 A.14), REQUESTED through `requestFealtyRow` under the `body:` prefix (PL registered it in round 4a), the episode `bodyId + ':' + <tick>` (BD composes it), requested only where `bodiesEnabled` ∧ `polityEnabled`; the liege's DEFAULT `hunted` (which every lawful court holds) is only its voice in this court's fork, never a price; a NEIGHBOUR's hunt is BD's pair matter (§10.2 (a)), never a fealty row .
- **The hearing `body_stance` (§12):** minted the week the court's FORK would change the standing word — its inputs the default above, the body's deeds in the court's land (a raid, a sheltering, a fight against a gang), a `supplicate` (a conclave's petition for leave or a shrine), a `bribe`, the liege's ORDER to hunt (PL's `huntOrderOf`, the precedence's call line — an INPUT LINE on this fork, PL §4.8 (2)), a war's need for irregulars (§9.8); and, from round 4 : the ALMS a far god's flock drew this season, weighted by the patron creed's share and legitimacy (§6.6); a flock's REFUSAL of a demanded sale (ST's `site_sale` `refused` moves the fork ONE STEP toward `curbed`); the court's own LESSON (HB's court row, §4.4 (a): a court that has curbed flocks drifts toward curbing the next); the LIEGE'S FAITH (a member curbing or outlawing a flock of its liege's own creed pays the liege's resentment and a step of the crown's protection, §10.2 (b′) — the mirror counterforce); the COMMONS' stance (a court hesitates to hunt a body its own people SUPPORT, §7.6); a LEAK that exposes a sponsor's hand in the body (the host court's fork is pressed to `hunted`, §10.4); and an OUTRAGE — a body that struck an envoy on public mission or a contracted caravan of this court (§9.5) pushes the court's fork to `hunted` and its bounty fork open (§9.9). A SEIZURE of a flock's site instead of a sale is the court's own choice: its fork turns `outlawed` (the flock hostile from that week, item 15) and ST's clearing follows (ST §6.1 step 6). DEFAULT: the fork's new word. The DM may leave, tilt (a bias on the fork's `net`, ±0.1 / ±0.3, DRAFT) or decide any word of the subject's line. After the court's act the CONCLAVE CHOOSES (`hide` · `defy` · `move`, §4.2) — the owner's "lie low · defy · move".
- **The covert hire (`employed`):** a court's `employed` stance is a CLANDESTINE HIRE on CH's ONE idiom (`covert: true`; CH §9 "ONE IDIOM, THREE TIES"): the employing court's secret, its leak a registered row of RN's `TELLING_SOURCES` (`leak:body:<bodyId>:<employerId>:<sinceTick>`, one per hire) whose CONTENT is CH's leak content `what: 'paragon_leak'` widened with `bodyId` and `bodyKind` (one meaning; CH registers the widening) , its exposure fired ONLY through CH's SHARED LEAF `exposeCovertHire(worldState, { subjectId, employerId, workedAgainstId, … })` (CH owns it; it calls `corruptionWeb.js :: applyForeignExposureBlowback` with the ONE source `covert_hire_exposed`; BD's stage calls it and never re-implements; called only where CH's covert arm is lit), ONE LATCH per hire — `exposedAt` on THIS stance row, written by BD's writer in the act that fires it (a row that stayed `confident` would otherwise re-fire weekly); BD NEVER writes CH's `paragonBeliefs` (the belief rows' one writer reads every subject's leak arrivals) ; ES-6a's leak risk the same. ROUTE CH (the idiom widened to a body subject; no second exposure source) · ROUTE RN (the telling row).
- **Patrols pursue only what their court marks `hunted` or `outlawed`** (and every beast); a `permitted` conclave is NEVER pursued; `curbed` is watched, not hunted (PR's round notices it; no pursuit) (ROUTE PR).

### 7.2 Body ↔ body — the landed labels, a prior by kind and by god, moved by deeds
- **Words:** the landed pair labels `allied · neutral · rival · hostile` (`neighbourSlice.js :: RELATIONSHIP_TYPES`), on `pairs` (one writer). A BEAST holds no stance toward anyone but `hostile` (BD10); every other kind holds `hunt` or `avoid` toward a beast — `hunt` where the band contest's odds favour it, else `avoid`.
- **The prior (written at first meeting, then moved only by deeds):** gang ↔ gang — `rival` where their radii share a road, else `neutral`; conclave ↔ conclave — by `stanceOf(god A, god B)`: `cooperation − aggression ≥ 0.2` → `allied` (sister creeds), `≤ −0.3` → `hostile` (rival gods), between → `neutral`; conclave → gang — by `stanceOf(the god, the villain's axes)` (§4.2): a GOOD god's flock `hostile`, a neutral one `neutral`, an EVIL one `rival` (the landed heuristic: "evil … forms no bloc; its cooperation is transactional and betrayal-priced" — it deals with the gang and never trusts it). 
- **Deeds move it (one writer):** extortion, a raid on the other's people, a fight → toward `hostile` one step; shelter, a shared enemy fought, a gift → toward `allied` one step; each an incident on the pair (≤ 8, the pair-state idiom); where the body is NAMED (BD23) the act is ALSO a deed on RN's ledger — `body_act`, its DOER the NAMED BODY (`{ kind: 'body', id }`, keyed `g:<bodyId>` or its creed's `creed:<deityRef>`), its leading paragon (CH's `leads`) riding as `ledBy` — one deed, one doer (RN §2.1), never "where a NAMED person is involved" . THE REGISTRATION (RN's route (d)) : THE ONE PERSON STAGE mints `body_act` from the hearing's CLOSING ROW — the resolved engagement SP-9's docket writes for BD's OWN kinds (`bodies_meet`, a `raid` with a body raider, `traffic_waylaid`, `body_stance`), `{ engagementId, kind, verdict, parties, where, tick, causedBy }` (BD-4's car-0 names the field path once SP-9 lands) — and BD REGISTERS its closed act kinds as rows of RN's inherent-value table under RN's registration walker (the `paragon_act` idiom: RN owns the derivation and the values, BD the closed list): the body's own — `raided` (a settlement's people) · `robbed_road` (a stretch's traffic) · `fought` (another body) · `gave_shelter` (a flock's shelter of the hunted or of a gang's victims; `sheltered` is the landed `DEFENSIVE_BANDS` word, avoided — `census-r5b.out`) · `merged` · `paid_quarter` and `fed_poor` (toward its own town's people, §7.6) · `robbed_quarter` (the same, the other sign); a COURT's PUBLIC act toward a named body — the `body_stance` words `permitted · endowed · curbed · outlawed · tolerated · hunted` (PUBLIC), never `employed` (covert: it travels only as its leak, §7.1) and never `harboured` (a derived fact, not a declared act); an ARMY's sack of a flock (§9.8 (c)). ONE ACT, ONE DEED: a patrol's clash with a body is PR's `patrol_clash`, a site's verdict ST's `site_verdict`, a hunt's success CH's `hunt_won` (B.5, §9.11), never a `body_act` beside them; a NAMELESS body and a beast mint nothing as doers.
- **Ties by status (the owner's "ties … appropriate for their status"):** a gang's ties ARE its stances and standings — a fence (its standing in a gate town with a merchant house), a watchman on the take (a `bribe`d court's `tolerated`), a lord who tolerates it, a court that quietly employs it, a SHRINE that shelters it (a conclave's `allied` pair: a gang may LIE UP at an allied flock's shrine — a tie, never a merge; "a conclave never joins a gang"); a conclave's ties are its stances (a court that permits or endows it, sister flocks); a beast has none.

### 7.3 A gang's standing IS its network, composed into the town's criminal power
- **Reads:** the gang record's `standings` (truth: its grip), the town's landed criminal power (`criminalStrength01Of`, the floor).
- **The composition (ONE read, read-time):** `criminalPowerOf(worldState, settlement, settlementId) = 1 − (1 − criminalStrength01Of(settlement)) × Π_gangs (1 − standing_g(settlementId))` — equal to the landed value EXACTLY when no gang stands there (dark: the function returns `criminalStrength01Of(settlement)` before reading anything), saturating, never above 1. Its readers become the landed readers: `supplyKernel.js :: buildSmuggleContext`'s `guildOf` (489 eff; one line), `espionageGauntlet.js`'s `guildEffectiveSecurity(…, criminalStrength01Of(targetItem))` (309; one line), `espionageMath.js :: covertCompetence01(homeSettlement, npc, ctx?)` (163; a defaulted trailing `ctx = { worldState, settlementId }`, its two callers pass it — PLAUSIBLE that `espionageMissions.js`'s call site holds `worldState`, BD-2's car-0 measures). The migration kernel's direct, cycle-guarded stamp read (`originCriminalGrip01`) stays the floor (a route to POP if it should read the composition). Leaf: `src/domain/bodies/criminalPower.js`.
- 
- **What moves a standing (one writer):** `smuggle` and a raid's `succeeds` in that town's land raise it (DRAFT +0.05, capped 0.6 per gang); a `bribe` raises it at the bribed court; time decays it (DRAFT −0.01 a week, the gang's absence); a LOST fight in the town's land, a `hunted` stance held a season, the gang's end lower it (the end removes it). DEFEAT MOVES STANDINGS (BD8): when the watch or the riders beat a gang, its standing there falls (DRAFT −0.15) and the court's legitimacy and the watch's reputation RISE through the landed verdict reads (`generosityUpdates.js :: applyLegitimacyDeltasToUpdates` — the one legitimacy writer PL's recognition uses; ROUTE INT/PL for the delta row, bounded DRAFT +2 points).
- **Felt:** "The Tolle gang's fences in Kell are doing well; the watch looks the other way." · scrutiny: the gang's network card (towns and grips).

### 7.4 A conclave IS the creed's row (BD3) — its choice leans the faith system's one strength seam
- **The seam:** `religiousContest.js :: advanceReligionStates` computes `strengthByRef[dref] = clamp01(pietyMult * deityLocalStrength({ … }))` per active creed per town, then `advanceShares`. A `cult` creed's weekly choice (§4.2) enters as ONE bounded multiplier on that creed's strength in that town (`× conclaveLean(worldState, cid, dref)`, DRAFT `[0.85, 1.25]`: `preach` 1.15, `rally` 1.05, `defy` 1.1, `hide` 0.9 — the covert congregation's growth is WF-5b's own half-life where lit, `supplicate` 1.0) and the COURT STANCE's bounded multiplier (`permitted` 1.0, `endowed` 1.15, `curbed` 0.85, `outlawed` 0.7, DRAFT) — one line in the host at the strength computation, the factors in BD's leaf `bodies/conclaveLean.js`. The SHARE then moves by the faith system's own law (`SHARE_STEP_MAX` 6, renorm to 100, `standingFor`) — BD writes no share, no standing, no legitimacy. 
- **A moving conclave's arrival:** `preach` at a town where its creed is absent calls `religionState.js :: attemptEntry(state, deity, newcomerStrength)` at the faith system's own bar (niche, capacity, `PUSH_MARGIN` 1.1, `EVICTION_MARGIN` 1.5), its `newcomerStrength` the body's size band's DRAFT strength × the court-stance multiplier; entered → the body ends `dissolved` and the creed is a `cult` row there (its flock's people are the row's share now); refused → it stays a body (and may `supplicate`).
- **Tier:** aspatial-safe for the in-town lean (the LOCAL lane); the mission and the moving flock need `faithSpreadEnabled` (dramatic and full) and the canon for the road; a deity-free world: dark (no religion subsystem).

### 7.5 The gate, not the curb — the balance of conversion (BD17; the owner's ~19:0x word)
- **Entry gated both ways:** the court's stance (§7.1) multiplies the creed's strength in the town (§7.4) — a `permitted` good flock in a good town grows at the faith system's own pace and its god ENTERS the town's pantheon by share; an `outlawed` flock's strength falls and its standing cannot climb.
- **The two stickinesses (measured, cited from WF's substrate):** entry into the ESTABLISHED set is share-driven and REVERSIBLE (`standingFor`; shares partition) — the natural cap; the PATRON seat is sticky by design (`PATRON_HOLD`, the flip margin, `resolvePatronContest`, `faithUnseatingEnabled` the only roads). BD never touches the patron's hold or the contest.
- **The balance:** a flock scattered or curbed loses its creed's share in that town (by the share law, through the strength lean), its standing falls back, and the patron's legitimacy rises through the landed legitimacy target (`religionLegitimacy.js :: deityLegitimacyTarget` already reads the town's faith context — BD adds no legitimacy term of its own); the NEXT flock's source is the NEIGHBOUR where the creed is established (the mission birth's odds, §3.2: share × piety gap × alignment affinity × the road, at a dwell).
- **The counterforce on curbing:** suppressing a creed that already has LOCAL followers raises their unrest and lowers the court's legitimacy among them — through the LANDED reads: the `religious_conversion_fracture` stressor's own gate (`stressorGates.js :: religiousConversionGate`) gains ONE `{ mult, reason }` row where the court's stance is `curbed` or `outlawed` against a creed with share (DRAFT ×1.3, "The faithful will not be told whom to pray to.") and the court's legitimacy target reads the fracture as it does today — "a town cannot hunt conclaves for free".
- **Power in institutions:** an ESTABLISHED creed is a temple institution in the town's institution count (the urban band's roster; "joining the pantheon" is concretely gaining that institution — PLAUSIBLE until BD-3's car-0 measures that the roster's religious institutions follow the creed's standing; ROUTE WF if the link must be built). The pantheon rank is read, never written by BD.
- **Felt:** "The Grey Lady's faithful came to Ashford from Kell and were welcomed; they keep a shrine by the mill." · "Merewick has outlawed the Grey Lady's flock; her faithful there pray behind shutters, and they are angry."

### 7.6 THE COMMONS' STANCE — a body's cover inside the walls (the owner, 18:43: "Go with your common's stance toward a boy." — the BOARD's verbatim; "a boy" read as "a body") 
- **The words and the one writer:** each town's PEOPLE hold a stance toward a NAMED body present in or near it — `fear · indifference · support` (`COMMONS_STANCES`, §2.6) — and it is NOT a second commons and NOT a second regard: it is RN's regard of the town's people (`s:<townId>`, RN §2.2: "the town's people, the commons") toward the body's key (`g:<bodyId>`; a flock's creed `creed:<deityRef>`), BANDED (`commonsStanceOf`, §2.5). It moves only by the body's DEEDS toward the town's own people through RN's door (the deed source `body_act`, a NAMED body's acts toward a town registered as rows of RN's inherent-value table — the SEAMS-r1-2 idiom: RN owns the derivation, BD registers the rows; ROUTE RN): a gang that ROBS its own quarter is feared; one that PAYS and PROTECTS it is supported (the owner's "good purchase" gang); a flock that FEEDS the poor is supported; a pack is ALWAYS `fear` (no key; BD10). INT's commons voice (the grievance ladder `petition · gathering · riot`) reads it where it needs to; BD writes no commons field.
- **What it does — the inside twin of the hidden ways:** a SUPPORTED body HIDES among the people — its COVER in its town's country rises (one bounded term on BD's cover input to THE PATROLS' encounter law, DRAFT +0.2, PR §8.1; ROUTE PR), and a HUNT there costs the court the commons' grievance (THE PATROLS' over-policing term `W_HARASS`, PR §7.5 — cite, never a second); a FEARED body is INFORMED ON — an `informer` sighting row in the town's belief of it (`believedBodyOf`, §2.4) each week it is near, so the court's belief sharpens and the hunt's prediction improves (PR's `predictedPlaceOf` reads the sighting); the stance also feeds RECRUITMENT (`support` is habitat, §2.5).
- **Tier · felt:** the regard arm lights where RN's regard does (`renownEnabled`; RN's person arm where `npcConsequencesEnabled` lights; omniscient presets derive it from the deed ledger, RN §8); "The quarter will not give up the Lantern Street gang; they pay for the bread there." (scrutiny) · "Low Ford sent word of the Tolle gang's camp before the riders asked." (surface).

## §8 THE EMBATTLED REGION — KEEP THE GAUGE, REPLACE THE ENGINE (BD-2; the owner's ~18:1x "this replaces our entire embattled region program where it becomes it and scales the size and numbers of events according to embattled region status and internal security?")

### 8.1 The gauge stays the one gauge
`embattlement.js` — its ramp (`rampThreat` in the frozen kernel), its hysteresis (`ENTER_THRESHOLD` 0.55 · `EXIT_THRESHOLD` 0.30 · `MIN_DWELL_TICKS` 6), its continuous level and its persisted ledger — is UNTOUCHED, a frozen host read by every landed consumer (road losses, routing, migration deaths, supply, the dossier, the map's danger legend (the host's H-rows unchanged), MN's composition). Occupation, siege exhaustion and plague keep their own feeders; no body enters them.

### 8.2 The bodies' row in the ONE presence composition
THE BODIES REGISTER ONE ROW, `bodyPresenceFeeder(T)`, into MN §3.3's `roadCountry(T)` part of `spatial/presenceComposer.js :: presenceTerms` (A.3's shared car — "a later feeder — THE BODIES' road-country row … joins by the same door and nowhere else"), gated by `bodyEffectsEnabled`, read where the canon marker is present. Per country T: the MAX over the bodies whose `where` touches T this week (a body on the stretch `a|b` touches both; a body on a trail touches nothing unless it SORTIED this week; a body at a site attached `at_hand` to T touches T) of `PRESENCE_BY_MIGHT[might] × ACT_FACTOR[act]` — DRAFT `thin` 0.35 · `modest` 0.50 · `capable` 0.60 · `strong`/`formidable` 0.70; `ACT_FACTOR` raiding this week 1.0, based in the country 0.8, passing 0.5; a beast in WINTER × 1.2 — so a `capable` raiding body latches a calm, unwatched country on its own (0.60 > 0.55), a `modest` one latches it only with the country's own crime, a `thin` one only sustains a country already embattled (above 0.30). The row sits on MN's SUPPRESSIBLE side: a held keep and the riders take up to the shared cap `σ(T) = min(0.25, siteSuppression(T) + patrolSubtraction(T))` off it (A.1) — a watched, kept country where a `capable` gang raids reads 0.35 and does not latch WHILE THE TOWN'S OWN RAMP `T` STAYS BELOW ≈0.31 — MN5's union `T + p(1 − T)` crosses `ENTER_THRESHOLD` 0.55 at `T` ≈ 0.3077 (MN's lane EXECUTED it on master's `stepEmbattlement`: `cure/D-MN-r4-probe/union.out`, "ramp T=0.3077 raw=0.5500 → … embattled"), so a town carrying its own war exhaustion or crime latches with the gang's suppressed row on top . The composer caps every row once at `PRESENCE_TERM_CAP` and takes the MAX over feeders, so a body at an ST den does not count twice: where an untied site's holder IS a body, ST's `sitePresenceFeeder` reads zero for that site and the body's row carries it (one term per thing — CARRIED in ST at round 4a: zero where `bodyEffectsEnabled` is lit, the record's might where it is dark, BD §15). 

### 8.3 The town's crisis follows the bodies — the raider stressor's gate (one row)
`stressorGates.js :: monsterRaiderGate` gains ONE bounded row (the `STRESSOR_SPAWN_GATES` idiom, `[0.1, 3]`, 609 eff): a body raiding or prowling the town's country this week → `{ mult: 1.4, reason: 'A gang works these roads.' }` (gang) / `{ mult: 1.4, reason: 'A pack hunts this country.' }` (beast) (DRAFT); so the town's `monster_raider_pressure` — the crisis the world already knows, its migration, its faith disorder, its counterforce — is born where a body actually presses, never in parallel (MN4's idiom: drive the stressor the world knows, never a second crisis). ONE ROW PER BODY per town (the row's `mult` is the largest pressing body's; never a product over bodies), and where that body HOLDS a den ST's own den row for that site YIELDS to it (ST's R2, round 4a — `gateResult` multiplies rows, so two rows for one den would double count) . The calamity bucket's beast WAVE stays the strike (MN11).

### 8.4 Scaling — bodies cause the level; the level feeds back as habitat
The size and the number of EVENTS in an embattled region are what its bodies DO (§4, §9): more and larger bodies, more acts; a region "scales" because its habitat breeds bodies (§3) and its security kills them (§9.3) — never because a multiplier on the level spawns events. The level FEEDS BACK only as one habitat term (a gang recruits where the roads are already lost, §2.5), bounded by the cap, the dwell and supplies. INTERNAL SECURITY (the watch, THE PATROLS' riders, a held keep under A.1) is the counterforce on habitat and the killer of bodies.

### 8.5 THE RE-FEED — a declared SAME-SEED SHIFT (THE PROMISE; OD-BD-2, the owner's to sign at its unit)
Lighting `bodyEffectsEnabled` (BD-2) changes same-seed worlds in every preset it lights in: the embattled level gains the bodies' row (countries latch that did not, and stay embattled longer); the criminal power read gains the gangs' standings (smuggling, covert competence and the gauntlet's security move); the raider stressor's births follow the bodies; and — measured in round 4 (`cure/D-BD-r4.md` m3) — the roads gauntlet's T3 arm (frozen `roadsKernel.js`) fires its `hostage`/`robbed` roll on more hops (it reads `embattlementLevel` against `ROADS_TUNING.EMBATTLED_THRESHOLD`), so more envoys meet bandit roads; and a GUILD WAR's condition (`guild_war`, §6.8 (d)) moves the town's criminal opportunity and defence readiness while it lasts  ; and — B.16 (BD-X2) — a BOUNTY's funding cost (one step of the court's effective band a season), a HUNT LED's detachment (PR's riders off their round — the unridden round) and its DEPLOYMENT upkeep, and the bounty's CALL on CH's board (which paragons and companies answer) all write OTHER programs' ledgers, so they light with `bodyEffectsEnabled` (§13), never with the record alone . The shift is DECLARED here, measured at BD-2's unit (a seed family's level distribution, latch counts and loss totals before and after, at the experience read), and SIGNED BY THE OWNER at that unit — never by delegation. With `bodiesEnabled` lit and `bodyEffectsEnabled` dark, no other ledger moves (pinned, §16 B2). Dark-after-lit: every row reads zero, the composition returns the landed values exactly, the level relaxes on its own hysteresis (no ghost: the row reads live records only).

### 8.6 Clock, tier, felt
Weekly, read-last/write-next (the composer reads the body ledger as the stage left it last week); SPATIAL CANON ONLY (the embattlement gate); the OMNISCIENT PRESET's arm the same (no belief). Felt: "The hill road has gone to the Tolle gang; Kell's grain came in short." · scrutiny: the country's card — "embattled: the Tolle gang (a strong band, hungry) raids from the ruin; Kell's riders keep the low road".
